**Sensitivity:** K1 wins 14/14, minimum margin 3.53. **Robust.** No numeric correction. (Rev. 3: every cell cited in scorecard-rev3 §7; K3·C4 3 → 2 because only one industrial product, Siemens IOT2050, is confirmed; K1 still wins all 14 runs.) The refuters' changes are to wording and to one missing gate (§5.5).

**Revision 2 (K2 steelman and a new kernel fact).** The independent K2 steelman argues for "K2-hybrid": a OneMind-configured kernel.org 6.18 LTS on every tier except Jetson, which keeps L4T. It scores it 70.0 against K1's 71.0, inside the tie band, and admits its C2 re-score is advocacy [steelman-kernel-runner-up §9 item 3]. Its strongest new evidence: HAOS uses one fragment set on 6.18.62 across x86, arm64 and Rockchip [C-STEEL-KRU-001, C-STEEL-KRU-002], mainline 6.18 drives the Pi 5's RP1 Ethernet, GPIO and USB [C-STEEL-KRU-004, C-STEEL-KRU-005], and Debian 13's 6.12 loses upstream support in Dec 2028, before Debian LTS ends [C-STEEL-KRU-009]. Its own weaknesses: HAOS still builds Raspberry Pi's fork for the Pi 5 [C-STEEL-KRU-002], and Debian's security kernel already tracks 6.12 stable (6.12.116) [C-STEEL-KRU-007]. **This file keeps K1 for launch** (frozen K2 = 66.0; with the steelman's numbers it would be a tie that the solo-operability and launch-time evidence still give to K1).

**But one fact changes the plan for the Secure Boot release.** The kernel checks module signatures only against its builtin and secondary keyrings [C-DEC-008]. A key enrolled in UEFI db, as X-2R did, goes into the separate platform keyring, which is not trusted for modules; only a key built into the kernel or a MOK key (enrolled through shim and confirmed at the console) is [C-DEC-009, C-DEC-010, C-STEEL-KRU-013]. So the first release that enforces Secure Boot **and** loads out-of-tree modules (NVIDIA on Pro boxes, DC-16 driver packs) has three options: (a) ship shim and enrol a OneMind MOK at the factory (console step per box, impossible for BYO boxes in the field); (b) build the kernel with OneMind's module key built in, i.e. K2-hybrid on x86 and generic arm64; (c) no out-of-tree modules under Secure Boot. **Decision: plan (b) for that release, unless T-W19-03 shows a console-free route that works on a stock Debian kernel.** This is the steelman's own trigger 3 ("W6 schedules Secure Boot with OneMind keys plus an OOT driver on any tier"), now met by DC-16 and DC-13. K2's other triggers (T-W18-3 patch latency, T-W18-4 a < 1 h weekly bump, a Pi 5 mainline boot test) stay as watch items.

**Note on the base decision:** K1 was scored with Debian as the x86 kernel. If S1 is ever re-opened toward Ubuntu, K1 becomes "Ubuntu's kernel + Raspberry Pi's + NVIDIA's" with the same structure; the route choice does not change.

### 3.6 Reproducing the corrected numbers

```python
W=[20,20,15,15,10,10,10]
def tot(s,w): return sum(a*b for a,b in zip(s,w))/5
def run(c):
    for i in range(7):
        for d in (5,-5):
            w=[x*(100-(W[i]+d))/(100-W[i]) for x in W]; w[i]=W[i]+d
            r=sorted(((tot(v,w),k) for k,v in c.items()),reverse=True)
            print(f"C{i+1}{d:+d}", r[0][1], round(r[0][0]-r[1][0],1))
run({'D':[3,3,3,3,3,2,5],'A':[3,3,3,3,3,3,3],'C':[3,1,3,3,4,3,4]})   # S1 corrected
run({'D':[3,3,3,4,3,2,5],'A':[3,3,3,4,3,3,3],'C':[3,1,3,3,4,3,4]})   # S1 rev. 2 variant S1-7 (C4 = 4 for both)
run({'RAUC':[3,2,3,3,3,4,3],'Mender':[3,3,3,2,3,3,3],'Rugix':[2,2,3,3,3,4,2]})  # S2 rev. 1
run({'RAUC':[3,2,4,3,3,4,3],'Mender':[3,3,3,3,3,4,3],'Rugix':[2,2,3,3,3,4,2]})  # S2 rev. 2 (S2-2…S2-4)
run({'C':[3,3,3,3,4,3,4],'D':[3,3,3,3,3,2,5],'A':[3,3,3,3,3,3,3]})   # S1 rev. 3 cited card (S1-9)
run({'C':[3,2,3,3,4,3,4],'D':[3,3,3,3,3,2,5],'A':[3,3,3,3,3,3,3]})   # S1 rev. 3 variant C·C2 = 2
run({'Mender':[3,3,3,3,3,4,3],'RAUC':[3,2,4,3,3,4,3],'bootc':[4,1,3,3,3,4,3],'Rugix':[2,2,3,3,3,4,2]})  # S2 rev. 3
run({'D':[3,3,4,3,3,2,5],'C':[3,3,3,3,4,3,4],'A':[3,3,4,3,3,3,3]})   # S1 rev. 4 card (S1-12: A, D C3 = 4)
run({'D':[3,3,4,3,3,2,5],'C':[3,2,3,3,4,3,4],'A':[3,3,4,3,3,3,3]})   # S1 rev. 4 variant V1 (C·C2 = 2, W2-HW-3 fails)
run({'C':[3,3,3,3,4,3,4],'A':[3,3,4,3,3,3,3]})                       # S1 rev. 4 variant V8 (D·G1 FAIL)
# The raw winner is not the protocol winner when the top two are within 3.0:
# apply the tie-break pairs of §3.1.6 (f): {D,C}->D, {A,D}->A, {A,C}->C.
```

Revision 3 output matches scorecard-rev3 §3.3 and §4 (its §10 holds the same script for S3–S5).

Revision 2 output: S1-7 variant, D wins 13 of 14 runs by 0.8–4.1 and A wins C7 −5 by 0.1 (inside the tie band in 12 of 14, the same pattern as the rev. 1 corrected card). S2 rev. 2, Mender wins 12 of 14 by 0.9–2.2 and RAUC wins C2 −5 and C3 +5 by 0.2 (tie in all 14).

---

## 4. The recommendation per tier, in detail

### 4.1 Base OS (D2)

- **Rev. 4: which base this section describes.** The decision of record is now **Ubuntu 26.04 LTS** (§3.1.6). Until the base is locked, the image definition builds both legs, and the bullets below describe the **Debian 13 (A) leg** as written in rev. 3. They stay valid for that leg, which is the pre-registered result if W2-HW-3 fails. For the **Ubuntu (D) leg**: x86_64 and generic arm64 use Canonical's kernel; the Pi 5/CM5 uses Canonical's certified Pi 5 image and `linux-raspi` [C-W2-057], with its open boot regression on older EEPROMs tracked [C-DEC-006]; recipes follow the rev-3 Ubuntu notes (name the initramfs generator, POSIX-simple gate scripts, never mask `systemd-logind`) [C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-001, C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-005, C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-008]; RAUC is OneMind's own build from the pinned tag on both legs (REC-DEC-31); support runs to May 2036 under Ubuntu Pro with no rebase, at an unpublished Pro for Devices price [C-W2-005, C-W2-006]; and **nothing Ubuntu-based ships before Z-01 is answered** [C-W2-050]. The Jetson flavor, GPU-server flavor and Industrial profile bullets apply unchanged, except that the Industrial profile is AlmaLinux 10 or RHEL 10 image mode, with CentOS Stream 10 only as a CI canary (it ends 2030-05-31) [C-REF4-DC-longevity-001].
- **Core image:** Debian 13 from a snapshot-pinned base, defined as a container build (umbrelOS pattern) [C-W2-043]. x86_64 and generic arm64 use Debian's stock kernel [C-W18-012]; Pi 5/CM5 adds Raspberry Pi's apt repo and `linux-image-rpi-2712` [C-W2-044].
- **CI hedge (rev. 2):** the same image definition builds `FROM debian:trixie` and `FROM ubuntu:26.04` in CI, and the x86 lab suite runs on both, so S1 can be re-opened by changing a variable (§3.1.5). **Rev. 3: proven runnable.** The Ubuntu leg needed about 40 lines of bootstrap change and no gate change [C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-011]. Recipes name the initramfs generator explicitly (26.04 defaults to dracut), keep gate scripts POSIX-simple (26.04 ships Rust coreutils), and never mask `systemd-logind` [C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-001, C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-005, C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-008]. Debian images include `apparmor` (k3s's check fails without it on Debian's kernel) and set locale, timezone and a first-boot policy so mkosi images do not block on `systemd-firstboot` [C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-016, C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-017].
- **Go/Rust components (rev. 3, DC-03 longevity refuter):** Debian gives Go- and Rust-based packages only "limited security support" [C-REF3-DC-longevity-001, C-REF3-DC-longevity-002]. OneMind's Go/Rust components (k3s, containerd, NATS, RAUC tooling where relevant) ship upstream-pinned with OneMind's own CVE SLA, never from Debian packages, extending the IIoT rule in §4.7.
- **Jetson flavor:** NVIDIA L4T Ubuntu 24.04 (JetPack 7.2+), which now covers Orin as well as Thor [C-W7-008], **for the first Robotics beta**. A Debian rootfs on Jetson is not an NVIDIA-supported option: CUDA 13.5 lists Debian 13 for amd64 and SBSA servers but not for Jetson [C-STEEL-jb-015]. **Rev. 2 changes:** (1) this flavor is a modified Ubuntu, so it is gated on Q-W2-1 like any Ubuntu image [C-REF-DC-cost-007 (SECONDARY: NVIDIA staff on forum), C-LIC-011]; NVIDIA's own BSP licence allows redistribution of the **unmodified** BSP on NVIDIA hardware with the licence text attached [C-LIC-001…003]; (2) **Yocto meta-tegra on `wrynose` with the linux-yocto 6.18 LTS kernel is promoted from "fallback" to "evaluated target"**: it is packaged upstream for tegra234 and tegra264, builds NVIDIA's out-of-tree modules against it, has an RT variant, and balenaOS ships Orin on it [C-STEEL-jb-002, C-STEEL-jb-004, C-STEEL-jb-005, C-STEEL-jb-010]. LAB-W7-6 runs inside the first 180 days with the jetson-base steelman's tests (CUDA and TensorRT within 10% of stock, CSI camera and NVENC on the LTS kernel, two consecutive 6.18.y bumps, A/B with OneMind's gate). It becomes the default only if all pass. Its known weaknesses stay on record: the LTS path is about four months old, its patch queue has two contributors, and meta-tegra itself is mostly one maintainer [C-STEEL-jb-004, C-STEEL-jb-006, C-STEEL-jb-007]; (3) every Jetson is flashed with `ROOTFS_AB=1` at the factory, because rootfs A/B cannot be turned on later by OTA [C-REF-DC-cost-005 (SECONDARY: NVIDIA staff on forum; the developer guide is silent [C-CIT4-017])].
- **Jetson support period (rev. 2):** per module, `support_end` = EOL of the last JetPack branch NVIDIA ports that module to. Plan for that date to fall **before** the hardware "available through" date: JetPack 4 ended 26–32 months before Nano and TX2 NX stopped shipping, with no advance software notice [C-REF-W7-T-001, C-REF-W7-T-002], and JetPack 5 ended on 2026-10-30 [C-REF-W7L-001]. Branch lives are uneven (r32 about 5.7 years, r35 about 4.5, r34 about 1 month, r38 about 5 months) [C-REF-W7-T-004], and security fixes land only at branch head [C-REF-W7-T-005]. Budget an in-field upgrade to each new JetPack major release **and** every security point release, or a paid sustaining contract after the last branch.
- **GPU-server flavor (Omni/Cluster):** permitted on Ubuntu LTS + RKE2, because that is the set NVIDIA validates (GPU Operator on Ubuntu; AI conformance on RKE2) [C-W2-026, C-W4-038]. **Gated on Q-W2-1:** Canonical's policy applies to *any* modified Ubuntu image OneMind redistributes, including this flavor and the Jetson flavor [C-W2-050, C-LIC-011]. Until counsel answers, the Pro box (single RTX) stays on Debian + NVIDIA's `debian13` repo driver + toolkit + device plugin, which works [05 §7.2], **with a recurring cost** (rev. 2): NVIDIA's DKMS build has broken on routine Debian point kernels [C-REF-DC-cost-009], so OneMind builds and signs `nvidia-open` modules once per image in CI and gates releases on a GPU smoke test, never DKMS on the device.
- **Robotics simulation/training GPU boxes (rev. 2):** Isaac Sim lists Ubuntu 22.04/24.04 and Windows 11 hosts only [C-REF-DC-robotics-013], and Isaac ROS on x86 is Ubuntu 24.04-only [C-W7-031]. These boxes default to the **Ubuntu GPU flavor** (after Q-W2-1) unless a lab runs Isaac Sim headless on Debian 13 and passes NVIDIA's compatibility checker.
- **Industrial profile (year 2):** one Containerfile building on CentOS Stream 10 / AlmaLinux 10 (free) and RHEL 10 image mode (paid, IEC 62443 path) with greenboot gating on k3s; Red Hat Device Edge for enterprise Orin buyers [C-W2-041, C-W2-042]. Fedora IoT bootc builds in CI as a canary with no support promise [C-W2-015].
- **Lifecycle plan:** no base reaches 2036-12-31 on one free release [03 P0-6]. Plan **one or two in-place rebases per box lifetime** (Debian 14, then 15) and test the rebase in CI from day one (T-W3-04). **Rev. 2:** on the Pi the rebase is paced by Raspberry Pi's kernel window, not Debian's LTS (security-only for the previous release "until we release Forky" [C-REF-DC-robotics-009 (SECONDARY: Raspberry Pi engineer on forum)]); Raspberry Pi does not support in-place suite upgrades [C-REF-DC-longevity-001], so OneMind's A/B rebase is OneMind's own tested work; T-W3-04 must record **founder-hours per rebase**, which is the missing number in DC-03. The Pi build pins an `archive.raspberrypi.com` snapshot and keyring and a tested `rpi-eeprom` release, never `default` [C-REF-DC-longevity-002, C-REF-DC-robotics-007], and CI has a 16K-page Pi 5 cell [C-REF-DC-longevity-003]. Do **not** plan on Freexian ELTS for a fleet unless an agreement permits redistribution to customer devices [C-W2-004]; ELTS guarantees only amd64, with other architectures "on request" [C-W6-023].
- **Rejected as the base:** Talos (no merged Orin support [C-W4-064]), Kairos (no Pi 5), SL Micro (support ends 2029), balenaOS (no k3s), NixOS and Alpine (short lifecycles), CIP Core (Pi 5/Jetson unproven; kept as the industrial kernel option).

### 4.2 Image build, updates and rollback (D1, D3)

1. **Engine:** RAUC on x86 and Pi 5 (custom tryboot backend; HAOS's script is the reference) [C-W3-066]. **x86 boot route (rev. 2):** target the RAUC **`efi` backend with one signed UKI per slot** from the first image, because it is the route X-2R proved under Secure Boot with no GRUB, shim or systemd-boot [C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-013], and choosing it now avoids a bootloader migration on fielded boxes later. The GRUB backend (lab-proven with a real k3s gate and 3 tries [C-LAB-rauc-k3s-gate-003, C-LAB-rauc-k3s-gate-009]) stays the fallback. **Condition:** the `efi` route gives one try per `BootNext`, because EDK II deletes `BootNext` before booting it [C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-003]. So the k3s-gate lab's "interrupted first trial boot is retried" result [C-LAB-rauc-k3s-gate-010] does not carry over automatically. A combined spike (efi backend + real k3s gate + power cut during the trial boot, with the gate re-arming `BootNext` while a trial marker exists and the slot is not marked bad) must pass before the first image ships; if it fails, launch on GRUB. Jetson: NVIDIA rootfs A/B + image-based OTA with OneMind's gate in NVIDIA's validation hook [C-W3-049, C-W3-050]; set `OTA_MAX_RETRY_COUNT` above 0 (its default end state is a recovery shell [C-W3-051]), and refuse to sign any Jetson payload whose layout flags differ from the device's recorded layout (a mismatched payload has needed a USB reflash [C-REF-DC-cost-004 (SECONDARY: forum report)]). **Rev. 3 (DC-07 refuters):** NVIDIA's image-based OTA ships an unsigned payload and leaves authenticity to the integrator [C-REF3-DC-security-001], so the **Jetson verifier is OneMind security code** under the same root as RAUC, reviewed and fuzzed like the gate; NVIDIA's rootfs retry counter advances only on warm reboots, so the Jetson gate turns every failure into a warm reset (watchdog or `reboot`) and never relies on a power cut [C-REF3-DC-technical-001 (SECONDARY: forum report)]; failover depends on the BSP point release (a watchdog bug fixed only in R35.5.0/R36.3.0) [C-REF3-DC-technical-002 (SECONDARY: forum report)], so each BSP gets a failover regression test, every OTA ends with a version assertion rather than trusting its exit status, and each production carrier board is qualified [C-REF3-DC-technical-003 (SECONDARY: integrator blog)]. T-W3-06 adds a cold-cycle-after-hang case, a mount-failure case and the version check.
1b. **k3s on a read-only root (rev. 2, lab finding):** k3s does not start on a read-only root out of the box (`mkdir /etc/rancher/k3s: read-only file system`). The image bind-mounts `/etc/rancher/node`, `/var/lib/rancher`, `/var/lib/kubelet`, `/var/lib/cni`, `/var/log/pods` and `/var/log/containers` from the data partition and writes the kubeconfig to `/run` [C-LAB-rauc-k3s-gate-004]. This also keeps k3s off `/etc`, which DC-16's confext needs (§4.8).
2. **Hardening the engine choice (from the refuters):** set `bundle-formats` to verity only [C-DEC-005]; ~~require RAUC ≥ 1.15.2 or a confirmed Debian backport of the CVE-2026-34155 fix, because Debian 13 ships RAUC 1.13 [C-LAB-rauc-002, C-DEC-004]~~ **rev. 3: ship OneMind's own RAUC package, built from a pinned upstream tag (today v1.15.2, `4fb7c798`) against the base's libraries, on every base.** Three lab facts force this: Debian's 1.13-3+deb13u1 does carry the CVE fix [C-LAB-rauc-1152-005] (the critic's N6 worry is closed), but RAUC 1.13 cannot install any bundle on a kernel ≥ 6.19 because Linux changed the dm-verity status line [C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-003, C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-010], Debian's package lacks that fix [C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-004], and upstream 1.15.2 passed the full real-k3s battery with `bundle-formats=verity` in two runs [C-LAB-rauc-1152-011]. The build is one meson call on Debian's libraries [C-LAB-rauc-1152-010]. Floor: upstream ≥ 1.15.2, or Debian ≥ 1.13-3+deb13u1 only on a ≤ 6.18 kernel; never a Debian 12 base (its RAUC 1.8 is vulnerable with no DSA [C-LAB-rauc-1152-004]). `bundle-formats=verity` stays as defence in depth: it refused a validly signed plain bundle, after checking its whole signature [C-LAB-rauc-1152-008]. CI boots the newest kernel each product may run (backports, HWE, vendor) and runs T1–T3, because a kernel interface change reached the update path; install `rauc-service` (Debian splits the D-Bus daemon out) [C-LAB-rauc-002]; set `check-purpose=codesign-rauc` if signing certificates carry the codeSigning EKU [C-LAB-rauc-009].
3. **Boot attempts:** RAUC's reference GRUB script gives one try, so a single interrupted first boot abandons a good update [C-LAB-rauc-006, C-LAB-rauc-018]; use **3 tries** as HAOS does [C-LAB-rauc-010], plus a hardware watchdog and `panic=` reboot. **Rev. 3:** 3 tries are available on the x86 GRUB route only. On Pi 5 the firmware falls back after one missed tryboot [C-LAB-rauc-1152-012], `boot_count` cannot replace a try counter because power loss clears it [C-LAB-rauc-1152-015], and the `efi` route gives one try per `BootNext` (below). So the update service treats an abandoned trial as **"re-offer automatically"** (download kept on `data`, bounded retries, never for a release the gate marked bad). HAOS instead drops the missed tryboot and marks the slot bad [C-LAB-rauc-1152-014], which is safe but makes an off-grid box download again; a gate re-arm with a bounded counter is the prototype alternative.
4. **One release contract:** one signed manifest per release listing a bundle per board family; one key hierarchy; one gate; one `onemind update` CLI/API/UI [04 V2].
5. **The gate (fresh signals only):** local kubelet healthz answers (probe the address it actually listens on; it was `[::1]` in our guest [C-LAB-debian-rugix-025]); node heartbeat newer than this boot; core pods Ready since this boot; 3 consecutive passes; data partition read-write; local OneMind API answers. Apps are not in the gate. A gate reading only API status committed a broken update in our lab [C-LAB-debian-rugix-023]; a gate that gave up on the first refused commit reverted a good one [C-LAB-debian-rugix-024]. **Rev. 2, proven again with RAUC and real k3s:** the fresh gate committed a good update after 3 passes while a broken app was ignored, rejected a k3s that would not start and a kubelet that never ran, and the naive gate committed the broken kubelet in about a minute in both runs [C-LAB-rauc-k3s-gate-006…008]. Three implementation rules came out of that lab: write the trial marker in RAUC's **pre-install** handler, because RAUC marks the slot active before post-install runs [C-LAB-rauc-k3s-gate-001]; read the version from the bundle manifest, not `RAUC_MF_VERSION` [C-LAB-rauc-k3s-gate-002]; never order the gate after `k3s.service` (k3s is `Type=notify` with no start timeout). Add an `OnFailure=` reboot for the gate unit in trial mode (a crashed gate is untested). Set the deadline at about 3× the good-commit time measured on real hardware (F1). **Secure Boot rule (rev. 2):** the gate refuses `mark-good` when the kernel's `onemind.version` differs from the root filesystem's release, because X-2R's exploratory E1 committed an old signed kernel on a newer root [C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-018].
6. **k3s and rollback:** k3s lives inside the read-only slot; its datastore is on the data partition; each trial boot runs against a copy; a k3s-minor guard refuses to start an older k3s on a newer datastore and offers the snapshot restore [C-W3-063]. The system-upgrade-controller is not used on image nodes because it writes into the host root [C-W3-065]. **Rev. 4 (DC-12 refuters, §5.9.2):** this is shown for **single-box SQLite** only. On embedded-etcd servers, rollback across a k3s minor goes through a cluster `etcd-snapshot` restore, not a per-node file copy (untested). App and core-service migrations are held until the OS commits. A gate failure where k3s reached health but a core pod was starved is an "environment" failure: it is re-offered with a bounded counter and an alert, never marked bad for good. After a commit RAUC's `prevent-late-fallback` retires the old slot, and security releases raise a version floor (both documented [C-LAB-secureboot-1152-010], to be tested with T-W3-03) (REC-DEC-45).
7. **Signing from release 1:** offline root, 2-of-3 release signers, signed manifest with expiry and offline grace, in-band revocation, CA rotation drill using HAOS's cross-signing method [C-W3-024]. Umbrel's *stable* release is the warning: it runs a server-supplied script [C-W3-034].
8. **No auto-retry:** after a rollback the release is marked bad on that device. (bootc's default timer re-staged a known-bad image in our lab [C-LAB-fedora-bootc-009]; the same mistake is easy to make with any engine.)
9. **Update default:** see §4.7 and `26-…` (CRA vs R-1, founder decision).

### 4.3 Disk layout (decide now; changing it later means a reflash)

This reconciles W3 (RAUC + GRUB, §11 of `04-…`) with W6 (Secure Boot later without repartitioning, SEC-01 in `07-…`, which assumed Rugix's systemd-boot flow [C-W6-049]). RAUC has **no native systemd-boot backend**; it offers an `efi` backend that boots EFI-stub kernels from two redundant EFI partitions [C-DEC-001, C-DEC-002] and a `custom` backend that can drive any bootloader [C-DEC-003]. The layout below keeps all three routes open:

| # | Label | Size | Content |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `onemind-esp` | 1 GiB FAT32 | **rev. 2:** on the `efi` route, only a **recovery UKI** at the removable-media path (never a slot-pinned kernel; SB-3); on the GRUB fallback route, first-stage GRUB + grubenv (two copies) |
| 2–3 | `onemind-boot-a/b` | 512 MiB each, **FAT32 with the EFI System Partition type** | kernel + initrd now; signed UKIs later, bootable directly by RAUC's `efi` backend |
| 4–5 | `onemind-system-a/b` | 10 GiB each, ≥ 10% verity headroom | read-only root |
| 6 | `onemind-recovery` | 1 GiB, reserved | empty at launch |
| 7 | `onemind-data` | rest | **LUKS2 from first boot** (recovery key now, TPM slot later) [C-W6-047] |

The FAT32/ESP-type choice for boot-a/b was **this file's design inference** from [C-DEC-002] in revision 1. **Revision 2: spike X-2R passed in emulation, twice.** On QEMU q35 + OVMF (Secure Boot build) + swtpm, with only OneMind lab PK/KEK/db keys enrolled, this exact layout booted signed UKIs from `boot-a/b` under enforced Secure Boot, updated A → B, rolled back an unhealthy update, refused a tampered and an unsigned UKI in the firmware and carried on with the good slot in the same power-on, and unlocked the LUKS2 data by TPM after every update and rollback, while refusing to unlock with Secure Boot switched off [C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-012…016]. The layout needed no change (each 512 MiB boot slot held a 200 MiB image with both UKIs) [C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-020]. **DC-18 moves from "design inference" to "lab-confirmed in emulation".** Four design rules follow:
- **SB-2 boot entries:** the installer creates both EFI entries, puts only slot A in `BootOrder`, removes firmware auto-entries for the OneMind disk and re-checks every boot, because RAUC's `efi` backend reports any slot listed in `BootOrder` as good, even an empty one, and OVMF re-adds its own entries [C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-019].
- **SB-3/SB-4 anti-downgrade:** Secure Boot answers "is this ours?", not "is this current?". Exploratory test E1 booted an old, validly signed factory kernel against a newer root and the gate committed it [C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-018]. So: no slot-pinned kernel at the ESP fallback path (a recovery UKI instead), the gate's version-match rule (§4.2 item 5), and SBAT generation bumps or db-key rotation on security releases.
- **SB-5 data key policy:** bind to PCR 7 **plus** a signed PCR 11 policy (lab bonus T7 unlocked across A, B, an unhealthy slot and rollback [C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-017]), built with a systemd initrd so the stub's policy signature is used natively [C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-005 (partial: documentation, not lab-tested)]. The DC-18 refuter showed that a literal PCR 7 binding turns every db/dbx/KEK change into a recovery-key prompt [C-REF-DC-technical-008, C-REF-DC-technical-009]. On owner-keyed boxes those changes come only from OneMind, so every OneMind key or dbx change re-seals the TPM policy first (systemd-pcrlock-style prediction), and the founder test applies a dbx update between A → B and expects no prompt. W6's X-2 command line with a literal `--tpm2-pcrs=7` alongside the signed policy is corrected accordingly. **Rev. 3 (DC-18 security refuter):** PCR binding alone does not stop the filesystem-confusion attack, in which a substituted LUKS volume with the same UUIDs makes the initrd fall back and run attacker code in the measured state; it extracted keys from systemd-cryptenroll setups, including one with Secure Boot [C-REF3-DC-security-009 (SECONDARY: research blog), C-REF3-DC-security-010]. So the systemd initrd also measures the unlocked volume's key into **PCR 15** and refuses to continue booting if the volume is not the expected one, with no fall-back to a passphrase that then proceeds in the measured state. A lab check of this (SB-5b) joins the founder tests.
- **Factory key enrolment** (DC-18 refuter): OneMind's db key is enrolled in setup mode at the factory, written down before the first x86 box ships. Boxes already in the field need an owner-run enrolment, which is not a reflash but is close to a service call. **Rev. 3 (DC-18 longevity refuter):** enrol the db key on **every** x86 box from the **first** shipment, even with enforcement off, because 2026's Microsoft CA rotation showed forced db updates leaving some machines unbootable or needing physical access [C-REF3-DC-longevity-008 (SECONDARY: LWN), C-REF3-DC-longevity-009 (SECONDARY: LWN)]. Before choosing an x86 ODM, a hardware test checks that an OS-initiated authenticated db/KEK update and a setup-mode PK enrolment survive power loss, and that the firmware does not ship a PKfail-class test key.
Real PC firmware, option ROMs signed only by Microsoft's CA, and a power cut during install under Secure Boot are still open: founder tests F-SB-1…3 on the Dell 5540. Pi 5 and Jetson layouts are as in `04-…` §11; Pi and Jetson fusing stays irreversible and only for OneMind-built hardware (07 SEC-04). Jetson "TPM later" is a factory decision, because its fTPM needs offline EK provisioning [C-W6-061].

### 4.4 Install paths (P-7)

| Path | Who | OS rollback | At launch |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 Preinstalled box | buyers | yes | when boxes ship |
| P2 Flash image | Pi 5/CM5 | yes | yes |
| P3 USB installer ISO (also rescue disk) | x86 BYO | yes | yes |
| P4 VM appliance (qcow2/vhdx/vmdk/vdi/ova) | try-before-buy, homelab, Mac dev | yes | yes |
| P5 NVIDIA flash + OneMind layer | Jetson | yes (NVIDIA A/B) | beta after T-W3-06 |
| P6 Platform mode on existing Debian/Ubuntu | developers | **no** | developer loop only; **unsupported after image GA** (rev. 2) |
| P7 Fleet / PXE | 100+ devices | yes | year 2 |

Why P6 stays developer-only: script installs have no OS rollback (Olares labels everything but Linux/Spark "not for production" [C-TD-olares-042]), and Home Assistant deprecated its script install modes in 2025 [C-W3-028]. Build order (W18 P0-8): the platform is developed first as P6 on plain Debian and in a VM, and the image is produced from the same layers from about week 3 [C-W18-062, C-W18-063, C-W18-064]. **Rev. 2 (DC-22 refuter):** every constraint-matched competitor that ran both paths later dropped the script one: Home Assistant ended Core and Supervised installs in 2025.12 because they "generate more issues" [C-REF-DC-longevity-004], Umbrel dropped script installs at 1.0 [C-REF-DC-longevity-005], and CasaOS stalled after its image product took over [C-REF-DC-longevity-006]. So P6 is a developer loop with a written sunset, and bring-your-own hardware means "flash our image on your x86 box", not "run our script on your distro". **Rev. 3 (DC-22 technical refuter, accepted in part):** about 1 in 6 opted-in Home Assistant users run its self-contained **Container** install, and HA kept it while ending Core and Supervised [C-REF3-DC-technical-004, C-REF3-DC-technical-005]. Those users are the developers who write apps and integrations, which the Market needs. The sunset therefore applies to **host-modifying** script installs only. A self-contained developer preview (the P4 VM appliance, or a container/VM bundle that does not modify the host OS and has no OS rollback promise) stays available after image GA, labelled unsupported for production. **Security conditions (DC-22 security refuter):** that preview is developer-only, not exposed publicly, has its own update channel and binds its dashboard to localhost by default, because script-on-any-Linux appliances have a record of quickly exploited auth bypasses (CasaOS CVE-2023-37265/37266, public exploits in 10 days) [C-CIT4-013, C-DEC-014]. Z-24 records the change.

### 4.5 Kubernetes (D4)

- **Platform node = ≥ 4 GB RAM and SSD/NVMe** (or industrial eMMC with a measured endurance budget). 8 GB is the recommended server size; 4 GB boxes run the slim profile and are sold as profiles (TAK node, greenhouse), not "run anything" [C-W4-002, C-W4-078]. Lab: k3s added +936 MiB at idle and +1,529 MiB under load on a 4 GiB arm64 VM [C-LAB-k3s-footprint-restore-007, C-LAB-k3s-footprint-restore-008].
- **Below that, or SD-only:** NATS leaf with store-and-forward (lab: 600/600 delivered after an outage [C-W8-050]) plus an MCP/MHS adapter; optionally a k3s agent on SSD. Leaf credentials are per device and revocable.
- **Node roles (rev. 2, security refuter):** a k3s *server* holds the API, the datastore with every Secret and an admin kubeconfig; Kubernetes assumes physically secure nodes [C-REF-W4-security-008]. Robots, drone companions and field kits that someone could capture run the **k3s agent only** (no server, no datastore, no admin kubeconfig on disk), enrolled with TPM-bound or short-lived credentials. Hub-class boxes run servers. A single-box home Lite is a server by necessity and relies on LUKS2 data (§4.3).
- **Trust roles per tier (rev. 3, DC-17 security refuter):** a Pi has no TPM, and k3s keeps its secrets-encryption key in a plain file beside the cluster CA [C-REF3-DC-security-008], so a Pi 5 is never the server or CA holder of a cluster that also holds GPU-tier or fleet secrets; `secrets-encryption` is on from first boot.
- **Entry SKU price check (rev. 3, DC-17 cost refuter):** 2026 memory-driven price rises (Raspberry Pi: 16 GB Pi 5 to $385 [C-CIT4-008]; NVIDIA list prices now $399 for the Orin Nano Super kit that launched at $249 and $3,499 for the AGX Orin kit [C-CIT4-009, C-W10-025]; rises of up to 101% on other Jetson SKUs per press [C-REF3-DC-cost-002, SECONDARY: press]) make the fixed ~1 GiB k3s overhead dearer. The minimum OneMind stack is measured on a real 4 GB Pi 5 and the entry SKU priced at current list prices against H-1 before the Lite tier is announced; if it needs 8 GB, the entry SKU is 8 GB or the smallest box is agent-only/leaf. The pressure is the same on every base, so the DC-17 row stays a tie.
- **Hardened profile:** every node runs a pinned OneMind k3s config (CIS hardening profile, `secrets-encryption`, an audit policy, `protect-kernel-defaults`), not upstream defaults [C-REF-W4-security-001, C-REF-W4-security-004]. The 4 GB + SSD gate is re-measured with that profile; if the Pi 5 4 GB leaves under 2.0 GiB or writes over 5 GB/day, the floor becomes 8 GB.
- **RKE2:** only when a buyer needs FIPS/STIG or an AI-conformant GPU cluster (RKE2 is on the AI-conformance list; k3s is not) [C-W4-038]. k0s is the named alternative for that slot and goes into the founder bake-off [C-W4-045].
- **Portability guard (TrueNAS lesson [C-DEC-007]):** apps never depend on k3s-only features; the Market compiles `onemind.app.yaml` to Helm, and imports Compose files, so the app catalog is not hostage to the runtime.
- **Version treadmill:** about 8–10 Kubernetes minor upgrades to 2036, never skipping one, each preceded by a datastore snapshot [C-W4-012, C-W3-063].
- **Ingress:** Gateway API with Traefik; ingress-nginx is unmaintained since March 2026 [C-W4-016].
- **DOKS conversion:** 13 dependencies, four of them real work, not "three changes" [C-W4-063]; plan in `05-…` §9.

### 4.6 Storage and backups (D5)

- **Storage:** OneMind storage class with `Retain`; k3s's default local-path class deletes data with the claim [C-W5-019]. Longhorn only on 3+ nodes with LUKS volumes; ZFS mirror optional on Pro, labelled "not a backup".
- **Single-box datastore:** online `sqlite3 .backup` + integrity check + token + node password + encryption config, all inside the same restic snapshot as the volumes [C-W5-009]. Lab: a full restore onto a fresh VM brought back all 32 pods with identical data [C-LAB-k3s-footprint-restore-011]. **Conditions the refuters added:** same k3s version, **same node name** (local volumes are pinned to it [C-W5-019]), app-consistent dumps for databases, and never a raw copy outside restic (state.db + token decrypts everything [refuter W5-P0-2-security]).
- **Clusters:** `k3s etcd-snapshot`, restic-wrapped, because snapshots are not encrypted [C-W5-013]. On restore to a new host, pass the original token explicitly; restoring only the token file failed in our lab [C-LAB-k3s-footprint-restore-012, C-LAB-k3s-footprint-restore-013].
- **Version binding and release gate (rev. 2, longevity refuter):** the vendor documents only "take a copy" of the db folder [C-REF-W5-longevity-009]; the WAL-safe online backup is **OneMind's** procedure, so call it "vendor-documented approach, OneMind-hardened procedure". kine is mostly one maintainer [C-REF-W5-longevity-006] and has known corruption reports [C-REF-W5-longevity-004], and each k3s minor lives about 17–18 months [C-REF-W5-longevity-007]. So: every snapshot records the exact k3s version (and the image keeps that binary fetchable); a restore runs on the same version first, then steps up; the integrity check **gates** the backup rather than only logging; and the "fresh box restore with verified data" drill runs in CI for **every k3s version OneMind ships**.
- **Datastore per box class (rev. 3, embedded-etcd steelman, §6.12):** SQLite stays the default **until** the corrected write test T-W5-03 runs (Pi 5 8 GB + NVMe, then an x86 mini PC; hardened k3s; 72 h; measured on the datastore device; starting ≥ 1 h after a fresh etcd install, not a conversion). If etcd writes ≤ 2× SQLite **and** ≤ 5 GB/day, hub, Pro and Omni servers start with embedded etcd (`cluster-init`), etcd ports 2379/2380 firewalled to loopback and the cluster's own servers, a refused two-server control plane, and an install-time fsync check; 4 GB slim profiles stay on SQLite [C-STEEL-etcd-every-box-015, C-STEEL-etcd-every-box-025]. Otherwise SQLite stays everywhere.
- **Growth:** SQLite → etcd in place with `--cluster-init` when a third server joins [C-W5-005]. **Rev. 2:** this path crashed into an empty cluster in k3s v1.32.5 and had no upstream end-to-end test until mid-2025 [C-REF-W5-longevity-002, C-REF-W5-longevity-003]; take a verified SQLite backup immediately before conversion, check object counts after it, and run the conversion in OneMind's CI per k3s version. kine will never produce etcd snapshots, by design [C-REF-W5-longevity-001].
- **Keys:** restic three-key repository (device, owner recovery, optional escrow) [C-W5-023]; emergency kit required to finish setup.
- **Verified restores:** daily `restic check --read-data-subset`, weekly scratch restore with app probes, "last verified restore" in the dashboard (W5-R7). Restore health checks use fresh signals, not restored status [C-W5-010].
- **Velero:** optional Cluster-tier app only, with a pre-created repository Secret (default static password otherwise) [C-W5-021].

### 4.7 Security and compliance (D6)

- **Day 1:** the §4.3 layout; signed releases; read-only root; k3s secrets encryption, audit, Pod Security Restricted for Market namespaces, default-deny network policy; mandatory owner credential at first boot.
- **Capability gateway:** human approval enforced *in the OS gateway* for physical actuation, OS updates, app installs and data export, because prompt injection has no reliable prevention [C-W6-068] and umbrelOS lets an MCP agent install an OS update and reboot [C-W6-052].
- **Robot runtime exception:** a signed, allowlisted profile granting host network, host IPC and `SYS_NICE` only to robot apps, because Fast DDS shared memory needs them [C-W7-033]. **Rev. 2 (DC-15):** those pods are host-equivalent, so the allowlist is enforced by signature (E2 in §4.8); Fast DDS's shared-memory failure is silent (discovery works, no data flows [C-REF-DC-technical-003]), so LAB-W7-2 asserts that SHM is in use; GPU-accelerated ROS couples the robot containers to the host driver/BSP (an L4T OTA broke an Isaac ROS container [C-REF-DC-technical-004 (SECONDARY: forum report)]), so an A/B update that moves the NVIDIA driver moves the robot containers in step. **Rev. 3 (DC-14 and DC-15 security refuters):** real-time robot pods need `CAP_SYS_NICE`, which Pod Security Baseline and Restricted both forbid [C-DEC-015], so RT robot workloads run in a dedicated namespace with a narrow policy exception granting `SYS_NICE` only to signed robot apps (E2), never a blanket PSS exemption; robot pods are **host-equivalent** in the threat model; Fast DDS on `hostNetwork` exposes unauthenticated, network-reachable parsing bugs on the robot's real interfaces [C-REF3-DC-security-004, C-REF3-DC-security-005], so robots use DDS Security or rmw_zenoh where the latency budget allows, and the DDS stack is rebuilt and shipped within the CVE SLA (Fast DDS 2.14, Jazzy's line, left standard support in Sep 2026 [C-REF3-DC-security-006]). **Rev. 3 (DC-15 longevity refuter):** RealSense's kernel package targets Ubuntu HWE kernels only and the ZED SDK lists no Debian [C-REF3-DC-longevity-005, C-REF3-DC-longevity-007], and RealSense changed owner twice in 2025–26 [C-REF3-DC-longevity-006 (SECONDARY: encyclopedia)]; LAB-W7-2 therefore runs a RealSense camera through the user-space (UVC) backend on Debian 13's 6.12 kernel and checks frame metadata, sync, multi-camera and IMU. If it loses a needed capability that Ubuntu keeps, x86 robot hosts move to the Ubuntu flavor; Jetson robot hosts are L4T anyway.
- **Real time (rev. 2, DC-14; citations revised by CIT4):** an RT kernel exists on every flavor, but no board vendor publishes a worst-case latency, and NVIDIA ships its Jetson RT kernel at "Developer-Preview quality" [C-CIT4-006]. On the Pi 5, the one peer-reviewed measurement found (PREEMPT_RT 6.6.21, 1 h, extreme stress) peaked at 124 µs [C-CIT4-004], while community reports on 6.13-rc RT kernels saw spikes over 1 ms overnight under memory stress [C-REF-DC-technical-001, SECONDARY: forum report]. On Jetson, two identical Orin modules differed by about 10 µs vs 90+ µs with full CPU isolation, which NVIDIA staff called "not expected" [C-REF-DC-technical-002, SECONDARY: forum report]; an integrator measured 6–15 µs with isolation and over 100 µs without [C-CIT4-007, SECONDARY]. OSADL's long-term latency farm has no PREEMPT_RT Pi 5 or Jetson [C-CIT4-005], so no independent long-run figure exists for either board. So the RT profile ships with a **per-unit acceptance test** (cyclictest for at least 12 h under k3s plus a memory/GPU stressor, with a stated latency budget), and **control loops tighter than about 1 ms default to an MCU**, not only "hard real time and safety".
- **CRA:** treat the OS as Class I [C-W6-011]; security updates installed automatically by default with opt-out, postpone and a window [C-W6-008, C-W6-019] (**founder decision**); declare support periods per SKU, capped by each board's kernel window; CVD policy and ENISA reporting runbook; route to self-assessment chosen by 2027-Q2 (§5.6). **Rev. 3 (CRA notified-body steelman, §6.13):** start a **parallel notified-body track in 2027-Q1** (a pre-assessment, or a RED type examination of the first radio box, plus a written quote; outreach drafted, the founder sends it), keeping the FOSS route primary. Candidate bodies were still in accreditation in late 2026 [C-STEEL-cra-nb-012, C-STEEL-cra-nb-013, C-STEEL-cra-nb-014] (no CRA notified body could be confirmed in NANDO; the count could not be read: UNVERIFIED, blocked [C-STEEL-cra-nb-017]; the Commission's page still said CRA listings would appear "once available" on 2026-07-31 [C-CIT4-010]), so the track also removes the late-2027 queue risk. The draft EN 304 626 accepts a documented update procedure ("KEVD or KEVA") where automatic-by-default is not chosen, so 07's reading that the standard requires automatic updates is corrected [C-STEEL-cra-nb-008, C-STEEL-cra-nb-020]; CRA Annex I itself still asks for them "where applicable" [C-W6-008].
- **NVIDIA modules and kernel security fixes (rev. 3, DC-13 technical refuter):** on Debian each kernel point release needs a working NVIDIA module build before it ships; NVIDIA's DKMS build failed on a routine 6.12 update [C-REF3-DC-technical-019], the GPU Operator has no Debian 13 driver image [C-REF3-DC-technical-018], and NVIDIA's `debian13` repository offers only the 590 and newer branches, still no 580 or 570 [C-CIT4-016]. Rule: a kernel security fix is **never held** for the GPU module; a GPU box may lag the fix by at most a stated window (7 days) with an alert, and more than one release-blocking module failure in 6 months moves the GPU tiers to the Ubuntu flavor (after Z-01), where modules ship prebuilt and signed.
- **CRA per SKU (rev. 2, technical refuters):** Commission guidance confirms that a free OS major upgrade without new hardware lets OneMind patch only the latest version (Art. 13(10)), but only once users can actually upgrade [C-REF-W6-technical-001]. So **D2 is confirmed for amd64 and conditional per board**: a board that cannot take the next base (Jetson modules frozen on an old JetPack [C-REF-W6-technical-003 (SECONDARY: NVIDIA partner)]) must be patched on its old version for the whole declared period, and its declared period is the minimum of OneMind's commitment and the board vendor's BSP window. Classification splits by what is sold: the **downloadable image** is a Class I OS (FOSS route, a fully applied cited standard, or a notified body); a **box** is probably classified by its single core function, not as an OS, and must be screened against the router, VPN, smart-home assistant and identity entries [C-REF-W6-technical-008 (partial: interpretation, needs counsel)]. "Pro" modules stay separate optional products; anything a box needs for its advertised use is part of the box. The FOSS constraint on D-001 holds "until a harmonised OS standard is cited and fully applied" (EN 304 626 was at formal vote in November 2026 [C-REF-W6-technical-007 (SECONDARY: standards tracker)]). Counsel confirms all of this (Z-05).
- **IIoT stacks (rev. 2, W8 security refuter):** protocol brokers and stacks (nats-server, open62541, Mosquitto and peers) ship only as upstream-pinned OneMind app images with their own CVE SLA, never as Debian packages: trixie carries 25 open nats-server CVEs and 23 open62541 CVEs with no DSA [C-REF-W8-security-001, C-REF-W8-security-003]. The 10k fleet test includes a fleet-wide trust-root rotation, because Rugix's compiled-in CA store needed an emergency binary update in 2026 [C-REF-W8-security-008]. The FDO item becomes "adopt go-fdo and fund interop testing": the C device SDK is archived [C-REF-W8-longevity-005]. CIP's 62443-4-2 update evidence assumes SWUpdate and Debian 12 + CIP kernel 6.1, so a RAUC-based OneMind image writes its own CR 3.10 evidence [C-REF-W8-longevity-007, C-REF-W8-security-004].
- **Public safety:** FIPS 140-3 modules (Go #5247, OpenSSL 3.1.2 provider), not RKE2's historical 140-2 certificate [C-W6-026, C-W6-028]; CJIS also accepts AES-256 under FIPS 197 [C-W6-033], which is the **day-1 answer**. **Rev. 2 (DC-24 refuter):** "rebuild Go components with GOFIPS140" does not yet work for k3s, which crashes under `GODEBUG=fips140=only` because of a PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA1 bootstrap key (k3s #13651, open), and fixing it needs a data migration first [C-REF-DC-longevity-010]. FIPS-mode k3s is `pending-lab`; the fallback is RKE2 on its active 140-3 certificate #4968 with Canal CNI and Traefik [C-REF-W4-security-006, C-REF-W4-security-007]. **Rev. 3 (DC-24 refuters):** "day-1 answer" is downgraded to **"candidate day-1 answer, pending the CJIS SC-13 text and counsel"**, and the regulated tier runs RKE2 until upstream k3s supports FIPS mode (refuter file `DC-24-security.md`). The data path is the real gap: Tailscale/WireGuard is "not FIPS compliant" [C-REF3-DC-technical-013] and the Go module's vendor-affirmed operating environments carry a key-strength caveat [C-REF3-DC-technical-011, C-REF3-DC-technical-012], so the public-safety profile wraps all CJI in module-backed TLS, or disables the WireGuard path for CJI. The base question is upheld: Debian is not the obstacle.

### 4.8 DC-16: plugin extension points the base OS must expose on day one (A-5, A-6, F-3…F-6)

**Question:** which extension points must the image expose in release 1 so that no plugin type ever needs a fork of the OS? (Full design in [`29-PLATFORM-ECOSYSTEM-MARKETPLACE.md`](29-PLATFORM-ECOSYSTEM-MARKETPLACE.md) §1; refuters in §5.10 below.)

**Answer (rev. 2): ten extension points, with enforcement built in.** W19 found nine [C-W19-047 (DC-16 conclusion, derived)]; the refuters added a tenth and turned four "reservations" into enforced rules. Most plugin types (apps, widgets, AI tools, automations) live in k3s and never touch the image. Drivers and OS profiles do, because the image is read-only. The Debian 13 VM lab showed that systemd system and configuration extensions lay a signed program, a udev rule, a settings profile and a kernel module over the read-only OS and remove them cleanly [C-W19-006…009].

| ID | Extension point | Release-1 status | Enforcement and changes from the refuters |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | Market/app controller reading `onemind.app.yaml` | **Frozen v1** | The public contract is `onemind.app.yaml`; Helm is a compile target and Compose is imported (REC-DEC-10). TrueNAS left k3s/Helm because it was "too complex and less reliable" for its users [C-REF-W19-cost-001, C-DEC-007]. |
| E2 | Capability broker for host grants (devices, host network, IPC, GPU, RT) | **Frozen v1** | Pod Security **Baseline** is the default; any host grant makes a plugin effectively host root [C-REF-W19-security-003], and the runc and NVIDIA toolkit escapes show the boundary fails [C-REF-W19-security-001, C-CIT4-014, C-REF-W19-security-002]. Host grants only for countersigned (Verified or higher) plugins, shown to the owner, removable live. |
| E3 | `systemd-sysext` / `systemd-confext` images on the data partition, matched by `SYSEXT_LEVEL` | **Frozen v1** | systemd's default image policy accepts **unsigned** images and plain directories [C-REF-W19-security-004]: OneMind sets a signed-only policy for sysext and confext and refuses directory extensions. One bad image stops *every* image from merging (systemd #32762, open) [C-REF-W19-cost-005], so `onemindd` validates each image in a staging area before placing it. A merged confext makes all of `/etc` read-only [C-REF-W19-technical-001]: use mutable/write-routing mode and keep k3s state off `/etc` (already required by §4.2 item 1b). Write a `SYSEXT_LEVEL` policy (what bumps it), because the wrong versioning axis forced a redesign elsewhere [C-REF-W19-cost-006]. Signature-policy bypasses were fixed in systemd only on 2026-10-26 [C-REF-W19-security-006]; check Debian 257.13. |
| E4 | Driver packs: signed kernel-module sysexts, built per kernel by OneMind's pipeline | **Frozen v1** | **Changed remedy:** W19 proposed one combined pack per kernel. Flatcar met the predicted masking in the field [C-REF-W19-cost-008] and fixed it with per-vendor packs plus a depmod-at-merge hook [C-REF-W19-cost-010], which keeps vendor release cycles separate. Adopt that, pending T-W19-02; the combined pack is the fallback. `module.sig_enforce=1` on **every** tier (unsigned modules otherwise load with only a taint [C-REF-W19-security-007]); the module key lives in an HSM and signs only in CI; per-pack revocation through E9; a written review bar for driver source. Kernel trust: a db key cannot sign modules for a stock kernel [C-DEC-008…010], so this is what moves the Secure Boot release to K2 (§3.5). Jetson has no NVIDIA guide for signing out-of-tree modules [C-REF-W19-technical-009 (SECONDARY, partial: forum; no NVIDIA guide found)]: Jetson driver packs are `pending-lab`. |
| E5 | UI slot (sandboxed iframes, MCP Apps) | **v1-beta** | Contract may change with notice until real plugins exercise it (cost refuter: freezing nine contracts before any third party exists risks freezing the wrong shape). |
| E6 | Capability registry + approval gateway | **v1-beta** | Same; the approval rules themselves (§4.7) are not beta. |
| E7 | NATS subject/account grants inside the locked grammar | **Frozen v1** | Unchanged. |
| E8 | Profiles (confext + sysexts + app set + defaults) | **v1-beta** | Same as E5. |
| E9 | Trust root: publisher signature, OneMind countersignature, digest pinning, revocation list, API levels | **Frozen v1** | Signing proves who, not what: VS Code kept shipping malware after signing, and removed names were re-registered [C-REF-W19-security-009 (SECONDARY: press), C-REF-W19-security-010 (SECONDARY: press)]; stolen tokens sign malware validly [C-REF-W19-security-011 (SECONDARY: security vendor blog)]. So: TUF-style **expiry and rollback protection** on the revocation list (an offline box past expiry refuses new host grants) [C-REF-W19-security-008]; threshold countersigning; permanent ID reservation; an appeal and owner-override path and a defined safe state per plugin class (false-positive kills stop working machines [C-REF-W19-technical-008 (SECONDARY: press)]); OCI-native signatures (Sigstore/cosign or Notation) under the same root as RAUC, because bespoke formats get retired [C-REF-W19-cost-002]; a costed review plan, since review is staffing [C-REF-W19-cost-004, C-REF-W19-cost-007 (SECONDARY: press)]. |
| **E10 (new)** | Boot-stage hardware enablement: device-tree overlays and early-boot drivers | **Frozen v1** | sysext cannot reach the boot partition or the earliest initrd stage (technical refuter). HAT/CAN/camera overlays ship only through the OneMind release pipeline, inside the RAUC bundle or boot slot, selected by profile. A vendor whose overlay cannot ship this way is the DC-16 flip case. |

**Rev. 3 changes from the 10 new DC-16 refuters (§5.10.1), all adopted:**
- **E9 v1 schema** adds signed configuration/profile objects, a rollout-stage/hold field, and a "disable capability" revocation type, not only "remove digest", because trusted publishing signed malicious bytes from a poisoned CI cache and sleeper updates fetched payloads off-registry [C-REF3-W19-technical-011, C-REF3-W19-technical-012 (SECONDARY: security vendor blog)]. Host-level plugin types are rebuilt by OneMind (or checked for reproducibility), get an egress allowlist and staged rollout.
- **Configuration is code:** a shared configuration that enables a legitimate plugin was enough to run attacker commands in Obsidian's REF6598 case [C-REF3-W19-technical-009, C-REF3-W19-technical-010 (SECONDARY: press)]. Arbitrary command execution is a declared capability that needs per-box owner approval, and unsigned configuration can never enable it.
- **Review is a pipeline, not a one-off:** in a sample of Omarchy's store one listing in five had moved past its validated commit [C-REF3-W19-technical-002]; OneMind pins digests on the box and re-validates automatically per digest, with human review only on manifest or grant changes, sized for about 100 updates a day.
- **The kill list is unproven until drilled:** HACS's `critical` file has held only a test entry since 2019 [C-REF3-W19-technical-004, C-REF3-W19-technical-005]; removal reasons are typed, "delist" and "disable" are separate, and a scheduled live-fire drill disables a canary plugin to its safe state inside the expiry window. The quality ladder's Bronze tier is fully machine-checked (60% of HA's own integrations are unrated [C-REF3-W19-technical-007]), and the version blocklist is part of the signed revocation feed applied before plugin load.
- **E9 lifetime:** a written root lifetime and offline rotation rehearsed on a box that skipped N updates; the box's verifier pinned in the RAUC image with the old format kept for the support window; a vendor-exit clause (owner re-rooting under physical presence, or an escrowed re-rooting key) so boxes outlive OneMind. Roots do expire on fielded hardware (Microsoft's 2011 Secure Boot CAs in 2026 [C-REF-W19-longevity-009]), and a trust-root format change has stranded old clients before (Sigstore's 2024 TUF root [C-REF-W19-longevity-008]).
- **E10 enforcement:** on Pi, the firmware loads HAT+ overlays named in the board's EEPROM and auto-detects cameras and displays [C-REF3-W19-technical-014, C-REF3-W19-technical-015], so images set `force_eeprom_read=0` [C-REF3-W19-technical-016] and turn auto-detect off, and overlays come only from the RAUC boot slot; on Jetson, UEFI-stage overlays need a bundle that updates the bootloader/`kernel-dtb` partitions or must be shown to apply at kernel stage. **E10 upkeep:** a per-kernel CI cell boots each certified overlay and checks its device nodes, because kernel bumps have broken vendor overlays for weeks [C-REF-W19-longevity-010]; "Works with OneMind" requires the overlay upstreamed or redistributable with a support term. **Rev. 4 (E10 refuters, §5.9.2):** the loader of the overlays sits outside the slot (Pi 5 EEPROM, Jetson QSPI UEFI). So the EEPROM/QSPI version is pinned, updated only through its own gated step and recorded in the release manifest; the CI cell is a kernel × firmware matrix on real boards; and the trial gate checks each profile's certified overlay device nodes. **Threat model:** on unfused Pi these settings sit unverified on the FAT boot partition, so E10 is a configuration control, not an integrity control, against local or physical attackers (REC-DEC-47).

**Image reservations from release 1 (all cheap now, expensive later):** `SYSEXT_LEVEL=` in `/usr/lib/os-release`; `/var/lib/extensions` and `/var/lib/confexts` on the data partition with a staging area; the confext write-routing directory; merge before k3s starts; the module-signing key in the kernel's trusted keyring (see §3.5); verity signing for extension images under the RAUC root; a boot-slot area for overlays.

**ADR-001 effect: D1 and D3 are confirmed with additions**, conditional on **T-W19-01's security variant**: with root on the data partition, an attacker must not be able to make an unsigned image or a directory merge after a reboot or a RAUC slot switch. If that cannot be prevented, D3 changes: host extensions ship only inside the signed slot (Flatcar's OEM model [C-W19-005]) and third-party host plugins are dropped from release 1 (founder decision Z-22). **What else would flip DC-16:** extensions fail to re-merge across a RAUC slot switch (T-W19-01); a required day-one device needs a vendor binary module OneMind cannot rebuild and sign; or the only working key route needs a console MOK step per device (§3.5).

Every P0 conclusion in W2–W8 and W18 was sent to **at least three** independent refuters with different lenses (revision 1 had two, or one for W7 and W8; the critic flagged this as S1-1); each refuter saw only the claim and its sources. Revision 2 also adds refuters for 13 decision-critical questions (§5.9) and for DC-16 (§5.10), plus one refuter on this file's own RAUC decision (§5.2). Files: [`evidence/adversarial/`](evidence/adversarial/). "Response" is required for every weakened or refuted verdict (brief §2 rule 7). **No conclusion was refuted.** Rows marked *rev. 2* are new. **Revision 3** adds 19 decision-critical refuters, so all 12 DCs that had dedicated refuters now have three lenses each (§5.9.1); 10 DC-16 refuters (§5.10.1); and two independent steelmen, embedded etcd (§5.4, §6.12) and the CRA notified body (§5.6, §6.13). **Rev. 3 tally: still no conclusion refuted;** of the 29 new refuter verdicts, 21 are weakened and 8 upheld (DC: 11 weakened, 8 upheld; DC-16: 10 weakened), and every weakened one has a response below.

Each refuter was given §3.1.6 (c)–(h), the frozen protocol and the cited rows, and had to find at least two new sources by looking for failure evidence. **Independence limit:** this session could not start sub-agents, so the three files were written one after another by one agent (FIX4) that had already read this file. |---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rev-4 S1: D (Ubuntu 26.04 + RAUC) wins the frozen top-two tie-break | technical / security | **weakened** | D's one-point lead is D·C3 = 4, tested only on Debian guests. `doc-only` is meant for missing hardware, and none is missing. A base difference has already broken RAUC on this exact pair, and Ubuntu 26.04 changes the layers the Secure Boot chain uses: systemd 259, dracut as the default initramfs, uutils coreutils with an open `timeout -k` bug. Our Ubuntu leg booted through GRUB and never used the `efi` backend [C-FIX4-008, C-FIX4-010…012 (012 SECONDARY), C-FIX4-014, C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-010] ([file](evidence/adversarial/FIX4-S1R5-technical.md)) | Refuted: an Ubuntu Secure Boot leg fails or needs a layout change (→ V3 → C). Upheld: it passes unchanged twice | **Accepted in part.** D·C3 stays 4 on the documentation reading of anchor 4 (lowering D alone would break rule 6). The basis of `doc-only` is restated (S1-12 note). **U-SB is pre-registered in (h)** and logged in `scorecard-weights.md` §8 before it runs. It is VM-only, so it runs before G1 with W2-HW-3. Until then the result reads "D, contingent on D·C3 = 4". *FIX5:* pre-registered and attempted on 2026-11-11, but **BLOCKED** because the Ubuntu archive is unreachable from this environment and the run did not bypass that. The kit is ready and moves to week 1 [C-FIX5-001, C-FIX5-002] |
| same | longevity / vendor | **weakened** | On the Pi 5, Ubuntu sets an EEPROM floor per release (26.04 needs 2025-02-11 or later; the fix offered needs a spare SD card), and EEPROM releases regress. Each Ubuntu-specific safety change (GNU coreutils instead of uutils) is a "modification" that deepens G1. Canonical certification requires a Canonical kernel, which may clash with the K2-hybrid fallback [C-FIX4-002, C-FIX4-005, C-FIX4-006 (SECONDARY: forum), C-FIX4-007…009 (009 SECONDARY)] ([file](evidence/adversarial/FIX4-S1R5-longevity.md)) | Refuted: trademark use tied to a Canonical kernel **and** no shim route (T-W19-03); or an EEPROM floor fielded units cannot reach in-band. Upheld: Canonical's terms allow a modified image on any kernel | **Accepted.** The kernel-tie question is added to O-1/Z-01 (§3.1.6 i). The in-band EEPROM duty is already E10 and applies to every base. GNU coreutils stays pinned on Ubuntu and is listed as a modification. No cell moves (C1 anchors count horizon and rebases, not floors) |
| same | cost / business | **weakened** | Canonical lists "Trademark use" inside Ubuntu Pro for Devices, so G1 most likely means a per-device fee on every box, including the Lite Pi. Debian and AlmaLinux cost $0 for this. A large derivative (Linux Mint) took a Canonical licence rather than rebuild. A provisional bound can be stated, and 01 had left it "unbounded" [C-FIX4-001…004, C-FIX4-013 (SECONDARY: LWN)] ([file](evidence/adversarial/FIX4-S1R5-cost.md)) | Refuted: a quote above about $25 per box per year, or Pro for Devices required fleet-wide. Upheld: trademark use granted at or below DC-03's $60 per box | **Accepted.** §3.1.6 (i) now states a provisional bracket of $0–$250 per box over 10 years, with its assumptions. The ranking is unchanged: C6 = 2 already prices this, and the G1 row counts for C. P4-8 stays with the founder and Canonical (Z-01, Z-39) |

**Net (FIX4):** no refuter refutes the frozen-protocol result. The decision of record stays **D**. It gains one pre-registered test (U-SB → C on failure) and a provisional G1 cost bracket. Confidence stays **low**. *FIX5:* U-SB was attempted and is BLOCKED (environment), so this net result stands unchanged (R4-16).

CUDA wording corrected: public CUDA is Linux and Windows; QNX CUDA exists only inside DRIVE, at 11.4 in DRIVE OS 6 [C-REF-W18-robotics-002]. MCU/flight-controller kernels are outside P0-1. |

### 5.6 Security and the CRA (W6)

| Claim | Lens | Verdict | Strongest counter-argument | Flip evidence (short) | Written response |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P0-1 (DC-04) rebase in place meets the CRA support period | security | **weakened** | Art. 13(10) covers *software products*; for hardware, Recital 40 says keep patching the latest compatible OS for boards that can't move; ELTS arm64 is "on request" ([file](evidence/adversarial/W6-P0-1%20(DC-04)-security.md)) | Commission guidance applying 13(10) to OS images on hardware; a lab A/B rebase 13 → 14 on Pi 5 and x86 | **Accepted.** The plan no longer relies on 13(10). Per SKU, the declared support period is capped by the board's kernel window, and a board that can't move to the next Debian is either patched by OneMind on the old release or covered by a quoted ELTS arm64 agreement (outreach draft needed). The rebase spike T-W3-04 becomes a gate. |
| P0-1 (DC-04) | cost / business | **weakened** | The "no additional hardware cost" condition fails when a vendor drops a board (JetPack 4 users were told to buy Orin) ([file](evidence/adversarial/W6-P0-1%20(DC-04)-cost.md)) | Adopted guidance that a base-OS major update is a substantial modification | **Accepted.** Same response; Jetson SKUs get the shortest declared period (§5.7). |
| P0-2 FOSS image is the self-assessment route | security | **weakened** | The boxes may not be "operating systems" at all; "fully FOSS" is impossible on Pi 5 (non-free EEPROM firmware) and Jetson (NVIDIA BSP licence) ([file](evidence/adversarial/W6-P0-2-security.md)) | Guidance on whether firmware blobs disqualify Art. 32(5) | **Accepted.** "Keep the EU image fully FOSS" is withdrawn as a hard rule. Replacement: keep all OneMind-authored code in the base image Apache-2.0 and keep closed features out of it (consistent with D-001 [C-W6-013]); get a counsel opinion on classification and on firmware blobs; choose the route by 2027-Q2. |
| P0-2 | cost / business | **weakened** | EN 304 626 will probably be cited before it bites; the FOSS rule may cost revenue for nothing ([file](evidence/adversarial/W6-P0-2-cost.md)) | EN 304 626 cited before ~2027-Q3 | **Accepted** as above; track EN 304 626 [C-W6-019] and budget a notified body as the fallback. |
| *rev. 2* P0-1 (DC-04) | technical | **weakened** | The Commission's guidance makes Art. 13(10) usable only when the next version runs on hardware the customer owns; Jetson is not on the Debian path and NVIDIA freezes old modules on old Ubuntu; Raspberry Pi does not support in-place suite upgrades; trixie arm64 LTS is "to be determined" [C-REF-W6-technical-001, C-REF-W6-technical-003 (SECONDARY: NVIDIA partner), C-REF-W6-technical-005] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W6-P0-1%20(DC-04)-technical.md)) | NVIDIA freezes Orin before 2032, or a lab shows A/B cannot cross a major base on Pi 5 or Orin (→ refuted for that tier); a written NVIDIA commitment, a three-board rebase lab and an arm64 ELTS quote (→ upheld) | **Accepted.** §4.7 now says "D2 confirmed for amd64; conditional per board"; the declared period per SKU is the minimum of OneMind's and the board vendor's commitment; the guidance (paras. 129–131, Example 52) replaces the bare article text. |
| *rev. 2* P0-2 | technical | **weakened** | Classification depends on what is sold: the image is a Class I OS, but a box is probably classified by its own core function (guidance Example 58), so the FOSS route is about the image, not the box; the box must be screened against other Annex III entries [C-REF-W6-technical-008 (partial: interpretation, needs counsel)] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W6-P0-2-technical.md)) | EN 304 626 cited and fully applied before the first EU sale, or counsel confirms boxes-only sales outside Annex III (→ no FOSS constraint); counsel reads firmware blobs as disqualifying (→ stronger constraint) | **Accepted.** §4.7 splits the image and box SKUs, adds "pro = optional extras only" (para. 145 vs para. 18), and labels the FOSS constraint "until a harmonised OS standard is cited and fully applied". Counsel question added to Z-05. |
| *rev. 3* Steelman: CRA notified body from the start | — | steelman | The only route that survives every open legal question (firmware blobs, paid editions [C-STEEL-cra-nb-002], the box's own Annex III category, a Class II "container runtime" reading [C-STEEL-cra-nb-007]); bodies are scarce, so queue early; robots and radios need outside labs anyway ([file](evidence/adversarial/steelman-cra-notified-body.md)) | Counsel says blobs or paid editions leave Art. 32(5); EN 304 626 not cited by ~2027-09; a Class II reading; a buyer requiring a certificate; a cheap micro-enterprise quote | **Accepted in its own "fair reading"** (§4.7, §6.13): a parallel notified-body track from 2027-Q1 (pre-assessment or RED type examination plus a quote); FOSS route stays primary. Its §8 weaknesses (no body to start with, change-control drag on a solo cadence, €20–150k SECONDARY) keep it from being the base case. |

#### 5.9.1 Revision 3: 19 more decision-critical refuters (each DC now has three dedicated lenses)

Each refuter saw only the DC answer and its cited claims. **Count now:** DC-03, 07, 09, 13, 14, 15, 17, 18, 19, 22, 23 and 24 each have **three** dedicated refuters with different lenses. DC-16 has its own 25 refuters (§5.10, §5.10.1). The other eleven DCs rely on the P0 refuters mapped in `evidence/decision-critical.md` (Result blocks), which reach three lenses for all of them except **DC-12** (no refuter tests the gate itself; it rests on six pre-registered labs across three engines) and **DC-20** (no refuter; decided by the DC-20 lab and the W3 steelman). Those two remain below the brief's "≥ 3 refuters per DC" and are listed in §12. Three of these files cite sources under REF3 IDs that the ledger assigns to other facts (DC-14, DC-22, DC-24 security); they are cited by file below, and the two load-bearing sources are re-ledgered as C-CIT4-013, C-DEC-014 and C-DEC-015.

#### 5.9.2 Revision 4: dedicated refuters for DC-12, DC-20 and E10, and round-4 refuters for the borrowed DCs

Each refuter saw only the DC's rev-3 Result and its cited claims. **Counts now:** DC-12 and DC-20 have **three** dedicated refuters each (technical, longevity, security); E10 has five (two in rev. 2–3, three here). DC-01, 02, 05, 06, 08, 10, 11 and 21 each gain **two dedicated round-4 refuters** (technical, longevity). That gives at least three lenses for each, counting their earlier P0 refuters, but only two refuters dedicated to the DC itself (§12). The new rows are ledgered in `claims/REF4-DC-technical.csv`, `REF4-DC-longevity.csv` and `REF4-DC-security.csv`.

| DC | Lens | Verdict | Strongest counter-argument (short) | Written response |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DC-12 gate | technical | **weakened** | The labs prove the gate, not cross-minor rollback. A per-node copy of an etcd member is a filesystem rollback that etcd does not support (k3s #3803). Apps migrate their own data during the trial, and rollback does not restore it [sources in the file, cited there under colliding REF4 IDs; not ledgered] ([file](evidence/adversarial/DC-12-technical.md)) | **Accepted.** Answer narrowed: safe design **shown for single-box SQLite**; on embedded-etcd servers, rollback goes through cluster `etcd-snapshot` restore (untested). App and core migrations held until commit. T-W3-03 runs twice (single box; three-server etcd), each with a migrating app (REC-DEC-45) |
| DC-12 | longevity | **weakened** | The gate excludes apps, so a k3s minor that removes an API a Market chart uses commits. "Never skip a minor" means about 30 signed stepping-stone images over 10 years, uncosted [sources in the file, cited there under colliding REF4 IDs; not ledgered] ([file](evidence/adversarial/DC-12-longevity.md)) | **Accepted.** Pre-publish API scan of every installed chart against the target minor. Stepping-path retention policy and its hosting cost (for 04 and the cost model). N → N+3 offline catch-up added to T-W3-03 |

W19 could not run independent refuters (29 §17). These ran afterwards, each seeing only one W19 conclusion and its sources. Every conclusion has ≥ 3 lenses except C-W19-011…028 (one technical refuter) and E9 (two).

| Conclusion | Lens | Verdict | Strongest counter-argument | Flip evidence (short) | Written response |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| same | cost | **upheld**, two caveats | One bad image blocks every merge (#32762, open); every `SYSEXT_LEVEL` bump invalidates all extensions [C-REF-W19-cost-005, C-REF-W19-cost-006] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-C-W19-001-cost.md)) | With verity on, one invalid image stops all merges and no graceful mode exists in Debian 13 | No response required; staging/quarantine and a level policy adopted. |
| same | security | **weakened** | systemd accepts unsigned images and plain directories by default; the merge source sits on the one partition RAUC never resets, so one root compromise persists across slot switches [C-REF-W19-security-004, C-REF-W19-security-006] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-C-W19-001-security.md)) | T-W19-01 shows unsigned `.raw`, directories and tampered images refused, and one refusal not blocking others (→ upheld); Debian 257 can't enforce signed-only (→ refuted) | **Accepted.** Signed-only policy, no directory extensions, and T-W19-01's security variant as the ADR condition (§4.8). |
