| same | technical | **weakened** | Items 3–4 assume OneMind builds and signs its own kernel; a db key can't do it on a stock kernel; no confext write-routing or overlay slot reserved [C-STEEL-KRU-013, C-REF-W19-technical-003] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-C-W19-049 (DC-16 conclusion, derived)-technical.md)) | A stock Debian kernel enforcing OneMind-signed verity and modules without a console step (→ upheld); only per-device MOK works (→ refuted) | **Accepted, and confirmed from source** [C-DEC-008…010]: §3.5 plans K2-hybrid for the Secure Boot release; write-routing and overlay areas added to the reservations. |
| E9 trust root | cost | **weakened** | Countersigning is a staffed promise; signed snaps still stole ~9 BTC; bespoke signing formats get retired [C-REF-W19-cost-004, C-REF-W19-cost-007 (SECONDARY: press), C-REF-W19-cost-002] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-E9-trust-root-cost.md)) | A costed review estimate fits the owner's time (→ upheld); signing didn't reduce malicious installs vs scanning alone (→ refuted) | **Accepted.** OCI-native signatures; reserved names and verified publishers; a costed review plan before the Community tier opens. |
| E9 trust root | security | **weakened** | Valid stolen credentials sign malware; removed names are re-registered; offline devices miss revocations (freeze and rollback attacks) [C-REF-W19-security-008…011] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-E9-trust-root-security.md)) | An E9 spec with expiry, rollback protection, threshold signing and ID reservation, lab-checked offline (→ upheld); no human review possible at volume (→ drop host-level third-party plugins) | **Accepted.** All four properties added to E9; founder decision Z-22 covers the drop case. |

#### 5.10.1 Revision 3: 10 more DC-16 refuters

Now C-W19-011…028 has 7 refuters (technical ×6 across its sub-claims, longevity), E9 has 4 (cost, security, technical, longevity) and E10 has 2 (technical, longevity; E10 did not exist when the first wave ran). All ten verdicts are **weakened**; every one is answered in §4.8's "Rev. 3 changes".

| Conclusion | Lens | Verdict | Strongest counter-argument | Flip evidence (short) | Written response |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C-W19-011…013 exact-digest review (Omarchy lesson) | technical | **weakened** | One sampled listing in five had moved past its validated commit; review volume scales with commits, not plugins [C-REF3-W19-technical-001…003] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-C-W19-011-013-technical.md)) | A costed automated re-validation plan at ~100 updates/day (→ upheld) | **Accepted:** digest pinning on the box plus automated per-digest re-validation; humans review grant/manifest diffs only. |
| C-W19-011…028 ecosystem controls | longevity | **weakened** | Controls rated "high" were never used, kept or survived incentives: HACS's kill switch holds a test entry since 2019; HA replaced a never-updated quality scale [C-REF-W19-longevity-001…007, C-REF-W19-longevity-012] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-C-W19-011-028-longevity.md)) | Evidence of forced removals at scale without harm, and a ladder-versioning policy (→ upheld) | **Accepted:** relabelled "code path, never exercised"; live-fire drill and ladder versioning added. |
| C-W19-014…016 HACS kill list | technical | **weakened** | One `critical` entry (a test) in seven years; free-text removal reasons; revocation in practice = delisting [C-REF3-W19-technical-004…006] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-C-W19-014-016-technical.md)) | A peer ecosystem whose remote disable fired on real malware with a measured false-positive rate (→ upheld) | **Accepted:** typed reasons, separate delist/disable, scheduled drill against a canary plugin. |
| C-W19-017…020 quality ladder and blocklist | technical | **weakened** | 60% of HA's own integrations are unrated; a version blocklist that rides the host release train is reactive [C-REF3-W19-technical-007, C-REF3-W19-technical-008 (SECONDARY: community)] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-C-W19-017-020-technical.md)) | Bronze fully machine-checkable; blocklist in the signed revocation feed applied pre-load (→ upheld) | **Accepted** exactly as the flip states. |
| C-W19-021…022 Restricted Mode / off by default | technical | **weakened** | REF6598: a shared configuration enabling a legitimate plugin ran attacker commands; no malicious plugin needed [C-REF3-W19-technical-009, C-REF3-W19-technical-010 (SECONDARY: press)] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-C-W19-021-022-technical.md)) | Command execution a declared, owner-approved capability that unsigned config can't enable (→ upheld) | **Accepted** exactly as the flip states. |
| C-W19-023…028 registry lessons | technical | **weakened** | Trusted publishing signed malicious bytes from a poisoned CI; sleeper updates fetched payloads off-registry [C-REF3-W19-technical-011…013] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-C-W19-023-028-technical.md)) | OneMind rebuilds for host-level types, egress allowlists, staged rollout (→ upheld) | **Accepted** exactly as the flip states. |
| E9 trust root | technical | **weakened** | Four recent failure modes pass every E9 check (legitimate pipeline/malicious bytes, sleeper updates, config-driven execution, store drift) [C-REF3-W19-technical-002, -004, -009, -011, -012] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-E9-technical.md)) | E9 v1 schema with signed config, a rollout/hold field and a "disable capability" entry, drilled (→ upheld) | **Accepted:** all three schema fields go into E9 v1 before it freezes. |
| E9 trust root | longevity | **weakened** | No plan for the root's own lifetime or for OneMind disappearing; roots expire on fielded hardware [C-REF-W19-longevity-006 (SECONDARY, partial: security vendor blog), C-REF-W19-longevity-008, C-REF-W19-longevity-009] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-E9-trust-root-longevity.md)) | Written root lifetime + rehearsed rotation, pinned verifier, vendor-exit clause (→ upheld) | **Accepted:** all three added to E9. |
| E10 boot-stage overlays | technical | **weakened** | Pi firmware loads EEPROM-named overlays and auto-detects cameras/displays outside the image; Jetson UEFI-stage overlays aren't reachable from the rootfs [C-REF3-W19-technical-014…017] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-E10-technical.md)) | A lab image with EEPROM read and auto-detect off and a Jetson bundle updating `kernel-dtb` (→ upheld); a day-one board whose overlay must come from EEPROM/UEFI (→ refuted, the DC-16 flip case) | **Accepted:** `force_eeprom_read=0`, auto-detect off, boot-slot overlays only; Jetson DTB path tested in LAB-W7-6. |
| E10 | longevity | **weakened** | E10 makes OneMind the permanent overlay maintainer for every add-on board; kernel bumps broke vendor overlays for weeks [C-REF-W19-longevity-010, C-REF-W19-longevity-011] ([file](evidence/adversarial/W19-E10-boot-overlays-longevity.md)) | A per-kernel overlay CI cell and an upstreaming clause (→ upheld); a closed day-one overlay (→ refuted) | **Accepted:** per-kernel CI cell and "Works with OneMind" upstreaming clause. |

---

## 6. Runner-up steelmen, summarised fairly

### 6.1 RHEL-family bootc as the base ([W2-steelman](evidence/adversarial/W2-steelman.md))
**Its case:** one Containerfile builds the OS as an OCI image; greenboot already implements "commit only when healthy" and did so in our lab [C-W2-052, C-W2-054]; `bootc switch` rebases without reflashing by design [C-LAB-fedora-bootc-015]; three suppliers on one format (Alma free, CentOS, RHEL paid and IEC 62443-certified [C-W2-042]); NVIDIA and Red Hat ship Jetson as a bootc image (RHDE [C-W2-041]). It re-scores bootc at 60–64.
**Where it is right:** on update mechanics and the industrial story, it is the strongest lineage; that is why it becomes the Industrial profile.
**Why it is not the base now:** the Pi 5 route is experimental and community-run by its own admission; the Jetson route needs a paid Red Hat subscription; NVIDIA's GPU Operator doesn't list RHEL with k3s [C-W2-026]; and our lab found greenboot rolling a healthy box back after a power cut in finalize, an open upstream bug [C-LAB-fedora-bootc-011, C-LAB-fedora-bootc-012]. **What would make it the base:** §3.1.5's re-open conditions.

### 6.2 Buildroot + RAUC, the HAOS way ([W3-steelman](evidence/adversarial/W3-steelman.md))
**Its case:** the most proven appliance stack (HAOS), tiny verity-checked images, chained 3-year LTS lines with no distro rebases, a fully enumerated SBOM for the CRA. Its own re-score puts it at 57 vs 59 for Debian + RAUC, a tie.
**Where it is right:** for an appliance with no NVIDIA userland, it is excellent, and it is the source of our engine, our signing pattern and our failure lessons [C-W3-024, C-W3-025].
**Why it loses:** no packaged NVIDIA container toolkit, per-board CI of 75–160 minutes, and nothing in the robotics/AI ecosystem targets it; those are exactly C2, C5 and C7 for a solo founder (M-6, M-7). **What would flip it:** a DC-20 lab showing materially fewer CVEs with < 1 h incremental builds, plus a working CUDA path on a Buildroot host.
**Rev. 3: the DC-20 lab ran** (same minimal x86 core with k3s, built with mkosi from Debian 13.7 and with Buildroot 2025.02.19 LTS). Buildroot's image is 4–6× smaller and its own SBOM is complete [C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-009, C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-010], and its incremental builds took 29–102 s, so **this file's "75–160 minutes per board" argument is withdrawn** for a minimal core [C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-008]. But it has only **7–18% fewer CVE IDs** (threshold: 50%) [C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-021], because the kernel carries 73–80% of IDs either way [C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-011, C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-012]; outside the kernel, Debian has triaged all 161 of its IDs while 32–53 of Buildroot's have an unapplied upstream fix [C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-013]. **The flip condition fails on the CVE clause; the CUDA clause was not tested. D1 stands, for a different reason:** the barrier is owning the userspace patch backlog (plus no NVIDIA userland), not build time. The kernel update cadence is the main CVE lever on either design (DC-21).

### 6.3 Docker + Compose on every box ([W4-steelman](evidence/adversarial/W4-steelman.md))
**Its case:** HAOS and Umbrel run Compose/Docker [C-W4-043, C-W4-044]; k3s costs about 1 GB of RAM and many times the writes of a plain engine [C-W4-078]; 8–10 forced Kubernetes upgrades to 2036 [C-W4-012]; NVIDIA's robotics tools and industrial edge platforms ship Compose; TrueNAS went back to Docker [C-DEC-007]. With G5 relaxed it scores 64.
**Where it is right:** on the smallest boxes and for device access. That is why the platform-node gate now includes storage, why 4 GB is a limited profile, why leaves exist, and why the Market must import Compose.
**Why it loses:** G5 (F-14): two runtimes mean two management planes and two app formats; the founder's working assets are Helm/Argo-native; growing one box into a household cluster is a join with k3s [C-W4-006] and a migration with Compose. **What would flip it:** a real Pi 5 4 GB on NVMe failing the budget, or the founder relaxing F-14.

### 6.4 Steelmen written inside the workstream files (not independent)
Kopia over restic (W5 §4.1: Umbrel's choice, Apache-2.0, error correction; loses on multi-key and append-only) · embedded etcd on every box (W5 §2.4; loses on flash wear [C-W5-018 in 06]) · K2 own kernel config (W18 §11.2; loses only on C1/C5) · keep Rugix (W3 §14.1; see §3.2). **Rev. 2:** Kopia, K2 and Rugix now also have independent steelmen (§6.8, §6.10, §6.7), which supersede these summaries. ~~Embedded etcd on every box still has only the in-file steelman.~~ **Rev. 3:** embedded etcd now has an independent steelman (§6.12), and so does the CRA notified-body route (§6.13). Every runner-up named in this file now has one.

### 6.5 Ubuntu 26.04 LTS as the single base ([steelman-base-ubuntu](evidence/adversarial/steelman-base-ubuntu.md))
**Its case:** described honestly, the Debian plan is already "Debian + L4T Ubuntu on Jetson + Ubuntu on GPU servers", so Ubuntu is the only base where F-14 is one vendor's fact. Canonical distributes CUDA in 26.04 [C-STEEL-ubu-003, C-STEEL-ubu-004], already has a 26.04 Jetson kernel with an RT flavour in `resolute-proposed` [C-STEEL-ubu-005], builds signed NVIDIA modules per kernel [C-STEEL-ubu-016], offers a free real-time kernel [C-STEEL-ubu-007], and has an IEC 62443-4-1-audited process with ctrlX OS at 62443-4-2 SL2 on top [C-STEEL-ubu-010, C-CIT4-012]. ROS 2 Lyrical is Tier 1. One security feed for a solo developer. It re-scores C4 3 → 4 and C6 2 → 3 for a total of 64–67 against Debian's 60–63.
**Where it is right:** G1 does not separate the two as cleanly as revision 1 said (the Jetson flavor carries the same question); DC-03 was ranked against an unknown price; G5 was overstated for Ubuntu but is not better for Debian. These are adopted (§3.1.3 S1-6, §3.1.5).
**Its genuine weaknesses (its own list):** the legal question is not closed, and shipping Ubuntu as the core on every box *increases* the exposure; Pi 5 is worse on Ubuntu today (LP #2170238 still New; the 2026-11-10 kernel doesn't fix it [C-STEEL-ubu-009]); no Ubuntu A/B appliance precedent and no lab run; Jetson is still 24.04 in practice; 26.04 ESM ends in May 2036 and the device price is unpublished; one vendor governs everything; 26.04's Rust userland churn; 26.04 ships RAUC 1.15.1, below the 1.15.2 floor [C-STEEL-ubu-008].
**Response:** its C6 = 3 is the one cell that would flip S1 (67 vs 63), and it depends on Canonical's answer, not on evidence we can gather. So Debian stays, Z-01 is asked now (it covers Jetson too), and the core gets a `FROM ubuntu:26.04` CI leg so the switch stays cheap. **Rev. 3:** the steelman's "no lab run" weakness is gone: Ubuntu 26.04 passed the full battery with its own RAUC 1.15.1 and an unchanged gate [C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-011]. Its "26.04 ships RAUC below the floor" weakness turned out to apply to Debian more sharply (Debian's 1.13 breaks on kernels ≥ 6.19), so both bases now ship OneMind's own RAUC and that row is neutral. Ubuntu now loses the head-to-head with Debian 7–4 (§3.1.5) and beats bootc 6–4. **What would make Ubuntu the winner:** Q-W2-1 answered favourably **plus** one of a priced Pro for Devices or the 26.04 Jetson kernel released with CUDA (§3.1.5).

### 6.6 Mender as the update engine ([steelman-updates-mender](evidence/adversarial/steelman-updates-mender.md))
**Its case:** the only S2 candidate that spans Zephyr MCUs (mender-mcu) to Linux to Jetson with one Apache-2.0 artifact format and one self-hostable fleet server [C-STEEL-updates-mender-001, C-STEEL-updates-mender-005, C-STEEL-updates-mender-006]; Orchestrator can update interdependent components over CAN or MQTT [C-STEEL-updates-mender-008]; the server supports every device API ever released [C-STEEL-updates-mender-010]; a 22-author server team [C-STEEL-updates-mender-004]; a published Update Module that wraps NVIDIA OTA on stock L4T [C-STEEL-updates-mender-012 (SECONDARY: community tutorial)]. Applying one evidence standard gives C6 3 → 4 and C4 2 → 3: **Mender 62.0 vs RAUC 58.0** (revision 1's RAUC score).
**Where it is right:** both cell corrections are accepted (S2-3, S2-4). DC-09 no longer counts for RAUC. Mender now leads the raw card by one point against RAUC's rev. 2 score of 61.0.
**Its genuine weaknesses (its own list):** open core bites exactly where F-11 lives: deltas, phased rollouts, RBAC, mTLS, Gateway and Orchestrator are paid [C-STEEL-updates-mender-015]; no revocation and no signing-time validation [C-STEEL-updates-mender-023]; no verity bundles, and Secure Boot via mender-convert is x86 UEFI only [C-STEEL-updates-mender-014]; `apt upgrade` on ARM "may brick your device"; Pi 5 goes through U-Boot; the stock-L4T recipe has unanswered failure reports; the server is heavy to self-host (11+ containers plus MongoDB) [C-STEEL-updates-mender-003].
**Response:** RAUC keeps the lead on the tie-break (§3.2). Mender's E2 (lab spike) and E3 (stock-L4T Jetson test) are scheduled; if they pass and either meta-tegra wins the Jetson flavor or a free delta path is measured, S2 moves to Mender. **Rev. 3:** E2 ran. Mender passed with the same gate and layout, needing no trial marker, and the engine-neutral layout held under both engines [C-LAB-mender-008…014]. It also revealed two facts the steelman did not have: stock Mender gives one trial boot (T9 abandoned a good update) [C-LAB-mender-013], and a gate script outside the artifact is skipped silently [C-LAB-mender-007]. The Debian-precedent row (umbrelOS shipped Mender) now counts for Mender (§9.1). RAUC wins 5–3; the flip now needs F3 (3-try patch) as well as E3.

### 6.7 Keep Rugix ([steelman-updates-rugix](evidence/adversarial/steelman-updates-rugix.md))
**Its case:** since 1.3.0 it accepts multiple signature roots, a key-rotation path [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-001]; v1.4.0 has a production LUKS2+TPM2 data driver proven by an upstream swtpm test across an A/B update [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-003, C-STEEL-updates-rugix-004]; it has a native systemd-boot flow [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-005] and the Pi tryboot flow Umbrel fields; downgrades can be blocked through component constraints [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-007]; an independent IIoT adopter exists (thin-edge.io/Cumulocity docs and meta-tedge, including a TPM2 Pi project) [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-009, C-STEEL-updates-rugix-010], plus RevPi and Strato Pi examples. Re-scored at 56.0, inside the tie band of RAUC's revision-1 58.0.
**Where it is right:** the TPM driver and the IIoT adopter are real and were not in W3's evidence. Rugix stays the documented alternative and the layout stays compatible.
**Its genuine weaknesses (its own list):** 168 of 169 commits in six months by one person, self-merged [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-019]; no succession, escrow or priced LTS [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-015, C-STEEL-updates-rugix-016]; latest-minor-only support with an out-of-date SECURITY.md [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-024]; GHSAs without CVE IDs; no revocation or encrypted bundles [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-023]; the Umbrel #2153 silent hang; about 47k lines of Rust to fork [C-STEEL-updates-rugix-020].
**Response:** RAUC's rev. 2 score is 61.0, so Rugix's 56.0 is now outside the band, and X-2R removed its systemd-boot argument (RAUC needs no systemd-boot for Secure Boot). **What would make Rugix the winner (any one):** a second maintainer with ≥ 10% of commits; written continuity or a priced LTS; sustained upstream contributions from adopters; or RAUC failing the Pi 5 tryboot spike that Rugix passes.

### 6.8 Kopia as the backup engine ([steelman-backup-kopia](evidence/adversarial/steelman-backup-kopia.md))
Summarised with its score in §3.4. **Its case in one line:** Kopia manages immutable off-site copies itself, keeps fleet devices isolated in server mode, is the data mover inside Velero and Kasten, and is Umbrel's choice; restic's extra keys cannot revoke a leaked key either. **Its weaknesses:** clock skew can delete data, a weaker format-compatibility promise, experimental error correction, no RISC-V builds, no native multi-key, no VolSync mover. **Result:** 71 vs restic's 75; restic stays; the two-engine Pi memory test decides any re-open.

### 6.9 One non-L4T base on Jetson: Yocto meta-tegra + linux-yocto 6.18 ([steelman-jetson-base](evidence/adversarial/steelman-jetson-base.md))
**Its case:** meta-tegra `wrynose` already packages linux-yocto 6.18 LTS (and an RT variant) for tegra234 and tegra264, builds NVIDIA's out-of-tree modules (including nvgpu) against it, draws its patch queue from NVIDIA's own platform patches, and balenaOS ships Orin on it [C-STEEL-jb-001, C-STEEL-jb-002, C-STEEL-jb-005, C-STEEL-jb-006, C-STEEL-jb-010]. It decouples a robot's OS life from JetPack branch EOL (JetPack 5 lost critical updates on 2026-10-30 while Thor modules sell to 2036 [C-STEEL-jb-011, C-STEEL-jb-012]), removes the Canonical condition, gives one kernel series across tiers, and permits one updater. Debian on Jetson stays blocked: CUDA 13.5 lists Debian 13 for amd64 and SBSA, not Jetson [C-STEEL-jb-015]. bootc on Jetson is a reference example still running L4T [C-STEEL-jb-018].
**Its genuine weaknesses (its own list):** the LTS-kernel path is about four months old with no evidence of camera, DLA or codec stacks on it; two contributors on the kernel patch queue and one dominant meta-tegra maintainer [C-STEEL-jb-006, C-STEEL-jb-007]; NVIDIA still calls its own kernel "the most complete and best tested" [C-STEEL-jb-016]; NVIDIA's container validation targets Ubuntu 24.04 only and NGC Jetson containers expect host libraries [C-STEEL-jb-019]; Yocto is the steepest tool for a solo founder.
**Response: its recommendation is adopted in full** (§4.1): L4T for the launch beta, meta-tegra renamed from fallback to evaluated target, LAB-W7-6 within 180 days on linux-yocto 6.18, every Jetson laid out A/B from first flash, and the CUDA/Debian and FF-2 wording corrected.

### 6.10 K2-hybrid kernel ([steelman-kernel-runner-up](evidence/adversarial/steelman-kernel-runner-up.md))
Summarised with its score and the new keyring fact in §3.5. **Response:** K1 for launch; K2-hybrid planned for the release that enforces Secure Boot with out-of-tree modules, because its trigger 3 is now met and a db key cannot sign modules for a stock kernel [C-DEC-008…010].

### 6.11 EL10 bootc for industrial SKUs ([steelman-iiot-runner-up](evidence/adversarial/steelman-iiot-runner-up.md))
**Its case:** for F-11, industrial and public-safety SKUs should run AlmaLinux/CentOS Stream 10 bootc, then RHEL image mode / Red Hat Device Edge, with Flight Control as the fleet plane from the first industrial release. On G4 that stack passes protocols, real time on x86 (AlmaLinux `kernel-rt` [C-STEEL-IIOT-003]), 62443-4-2 on the OS itself (RHEL 9.6 / MicroShift 4.21, TÜV Rheinland [C-STEEL-IIOT-008]) and zero-touch onboarding (go-fdo client and server packaged in CentOS Stream 10 and AlmaLinux 10 [C-STEEL-IIOT-001, C-STEEL-IIOT-002]; TPM device identity in Flight Control [C-STEEL-IIOT-006]); only fleet scale stays conditional. Debian is conditional on three of those.
**Its genuine weaknesses (its own list):** DC-02 is not flipped (every Debian gap is CONDITIONAL with a costed mitigation); Pi 5 bootc is experimental, so greenhouse SKUs would still need Debian and OneMind would run two bases; open62541 is not in EPEL 10; no 10k evidence either; the certificate covers RHEL 9.6, not RHEL 10, AlmaLinux or k3s; AlmaLinux is binary-compatible, not identical, after Red Hat's 2023 source change.
**Response:** the decided plan stands (Debian + RAUC + k3s; bootc Industrial profile in year 2). Two of its tests are pulled into the 180-day plan because they are cheap and decisive: LAB-W8-4 FDO onboarding on **both** stacks, and a Flight Control `devicesimulator` run [C-STEEL-IIOT-007] beside the RAUC fleet test. **What would make it the winner:** both of those favour EL10, plus one of a supported Pi 5 bootc, an RFP needing an OS-level 62443 certificate, a materially cheaper assessor quote, or Debian RT failing LAB-W7-1.

### 6.12 Embedded etcd on every k3s server ([steelman-etcd-every-box](evidence/adversarial/steelman-etcd-every-box.md)) *(rev. 3)*
**Its case:** one datastore means one backup format, one restore path and no one-way `--cluster-init` conversion, which crashed into an empty cluster in k3s v1.32.5 [C-REF-W5-longevity-002]; kine, the SQLite shim, is titled "Kine is not etcd" in its own README and is mostly one maintainer, while etcd is a multi-company Kubernetes project [C-STEEL-etcd-every-box-001…012]; our 8.7× write figure came from a 10-minute window seconds after a conversion, while k3s's own profile shows etcd using half SQLite's bandwidth; even 8× would not wear out a common NVMe in ten years, and §4.5 already requires an SSD on every server.
**Where it is right:** the conversion risk and the second restore path are real costs for boxes likely to grow into a cluster.
**Its genuine weaknesses (its own list):** our only head-to-head still says 8.7×, and an extrapolated ~25 GB/day would fail §4.5's own 5 GB/day gate; etcd listens on ports 2379/2380 on the node address even on one box [C-STEEL-etcd-every-box-015]; its restore failed the first time in our lab until the token was passed explicitly [C-LAB-k3s-footprint-restore-013]; it punishes slow disks [C-STEEL-etcd-every-box-013]; a two-server etcd is *less* available than one; more RAM on 4 GB boxes.
**Response:** adopted in its own strongest form, conditionally (§4.6): hub, Pro and Omni servers start on etcd if corrected T-W5-03 passes; 4 GB slim profiles stay SQLite. Its tenth point, kine's NATS JetStream driver [C-STEEL-etcd-every-box-005], is noted as an unexplored third option with no lab evidence.

### 6.13 CRA notified body from the start ([steelman-cra-notified-body](evidence/adversarial/steelman-cra-notified-body.md)) *(rev. 3)*
**Its case:** third-party assessment (module B+C, then H) is the only CRA route that survives every open legal question: firmware blobs, a paid edition [C-STEEL-cra-nb-002], the box's own Annex III category, and a Class II "container runtime" reading of a k3s appliance [C-STEEL-cra-nb-007]. Bodies were still in accreditation in late 2026 [C-STEEL-cra-nb-011…014], so early queuing matters; robots (Machinery Regulation) and radios (RED) will need outside labs anyway, and RED type-examination certificates can carry over [C-STEEL-cra-nb-006].
**Where it is right:** waiting until 2027-Q2 to decide leaves no time if counsel rules against the FOSS route.
**Its genuine weaknesses (its own list):** nothing can be certified before a body is designated; the free routes may well open (draft EN 304 626 accepts a documented update procedure [C-STEEL-cra-nb-008, C-STEEL-cra-nb-020]); the box probably needs no body (Example 58 [C-STEEL-cra-nb-004]); change control drags on a solo release cadence; €20–150k (SECONDARY); it removes no manufacturer duty.
**Response:** adopted in its own "fair reading" (§4.7): a parallel track from 2027-Q1 (pre-assessment or RED type examination, plus a quote), FOSS route primary. Z-05 records it.

---

### 6.14 RHEL-family bootc as the OneMind core ([STEEL4-bootc-core](evidence/adversarial/STEEL4-bootc-core.md)) *(rev. 4)*

**Case:** the OS is an OCI image, so one artefact, registry and signing chain serves both the OS and the apps, and logically bound images roll the core pods back with the OS [C-STEEL4-bootc-022]. Rebase is a built-in verb (lab-shown [C-LAB-fedora-bootc-015]). Offline USB updates are built in [C-STEEL4-bootc-021]. AlmaLinux 10 has a dated end (2035-05-31) and needs no permission to redistribute [C-W2-013, C-STEEL4-bootc-014]. Its Pi bootloader script is in fact BSD-2/Apache-2.0, which corrects C-SCORE-026's "no licence" [C-STEEL4-bootc-006]. Composefs sealing gives a higher security ceiling [C-STEEL4-bootc-018]. Several vendors ship the update stack, which is a CNCF project [C-STEEL4-bootc-017]. Red Hat promises Day-0 NVIDIA support on RHEL [C-STEEL4-bootc-011]. **Weak spots it states itself:** the Pi 5 path is one person's repo (75 commits, 12 stars) [C-STEEL4-bootc-001]; the Pi commit is manual and unpinned [C-STEEL4-bootc-003]; the free Jetson route is frozen at RHEL 9.4 / r36.3.1 [C-STEEL4-bootc-013]; #219 is unfixed [C-STEEL4-bootc-024]; sealing and boot counting are not in the same backend yet [C-STEEL4-bootc-019, C-STEEL4-bootc-025]. **Its scorecard reading:** no cell up; C·C2 = 3 is defensible but fragile; C3 stays 3. **Its protocol reading:** under the frozen rules, C is the winner or a finalist, never A. **Response (rev. 4):** accepted on protocol (§3.1.6). The point the run had missed is accepted too: **the choice expires at first shipment**, because Debian/Ubuntu + RAUC and bootc differ in layout, bootloader and client. W2-HW-3 therefore moves before G1 (REC-DEC-44). Its flip conditions 4 (two or more Pi maintainers, or OneMind owns the repo) and 5 (composefs + systemd-boot counting spike) are added to the map's "W2-HW-3 passes" row. The tier-argument critique stands, since that argument is withdrawn.

---

What can still move a verdict is named per row as a test or a founder decision, with its trigger written in advance (§3.1.5, §3.2, §4.6, §12).

**Net: ADR-001 is confirmed in architecture and changed in many specifics; no element of its core (own image, Debian core, A/B with health gate, k3s, restic) is overturned.** Revision 2 confirms that again, but the Debian and RAUC choices are now tie-break wins, not scorecard wins, and each has a written path back to Ubuntu or Mender.

**Final net (revision 3):** confirmed — D1 (own image), D2 (Debian core), D3 (A/B, signed, fresh-signal gate), D4 (k3s on platform nodes), D5 (restic); changed — the update engine (Rugix → RAUC, OneMind-built), the datastore method, the RKE2/FIPS text, the CRA route timing, Secure Boot timing (layout day 1, enforcement later, factory enrolment for all x86); overturned — Kairos for Jetson, system-upgrade-controller on image nodes, "DOKS in three changes", "no Fedora/RHEL role". The two lead choices (Debian, RAUC) were tested on **equal evidence** in rev. 3 for the first time and survived; they remain tie-break wins with pre-registered triggers, and confidence stays **low-medium (base)** and **medium-low (engine)**.

### 7.3 What revision 4 changed

See **"What changed in revision 4"** at the top of this file (R4-1…R4-14). For ADR-001: **D2's Debian 13 core is not the frozen protocol's result.** The decision of record is Ubuntu 26.04 LTS + RAUC, conditional on Z-01 and G5, with Debian as the pre-registered result if W2-HW-3 fails, and bootc if Canonical refuses or if the Ubuntu Secure Boot leg U-SB fails (FIX4). **D1, D3, D4, D5 and D6 are unchanged in direction.** D3's gate is narrowed to single-box SQLite until T-W3-03 runs on a three-server cluster. D6 gains the shim route as T-W19-03's first arm.

**Final net (revision 4):** confirmed: D1 (own image; distro base, now on CUDA and maintainability grounds), D3 (A/B, signed, fresh-signal gate, RAUC), D4 (k3s on platform nodes), D5 (restic). **Changed (conditional): D2 core base, Debian 13 → Ubuntu 26.04 LTS by the frozen protocol**, fragile and gated on Z-01. Overturned: as in rev. 3. This is the first revision in which ADR-001's base-OS choice is not the scorecard's result. It may still become the shipping base through the pre-registered map or a logged founder decision.

## 8. P-3 · OneMind mapped onto Olares's layers

Olares organises itself in five layers: Infrastructure → Platform → Framework → Built-in apps → Community apps (`02-competitors/olares.md` §1). OneMind keeps that shape and adds two layers Olares does not have: an **owned OS** underneath, and a **device and robot** layer beside the platform.

| Olares layer (what Olares runs) | OneMind equivalent | What OneMind does differently, and why |
|---|---|---|
| *(none: rides on a host Ubuntu/Debian; the ISO installs Ubuntu)* [C-TD-olares-007] | **L0 · OneMind OS image:** Debian 13 read-only A/B (RAUC), signed releases, health gate, LUKS2 data; Jetson flavor on L4T | Olares has no OS rollback and no release signatures beyond same-origin checksums [C-TD-olares-003, C-TD-olares-020]; owning the image is what makes updates safe (R-1, R-3) |
| **Infrastructure:** k3s v1.33.3 with external etcd, Calico, OpenEBS, HAMi [C-TD-olares-021, C-TD-olares-023] | **L1 · Cluster:** k3s (SQLite on one box, etcd at 3 servers), Flannel (Cilium on GPU/enterprise), local-path Retain / Longhorn, capability mapping to device plugins and DRA, HAMi optional | Olares ships a Kubernetes minor past its end of life [C-TD-olares-022]; OneMind tracks supported minors and snapshots before each upgrade (N-9, R-3) |
| **Platform:** Postgres/Citus, KVRocks, NATS, JuiceFS/MinIO, KubeBlocks | **L2 · Platform services:** NATS JetStream as the authority bus (founder's doctrine), object storage (Garage/MinIO), backup agent (restic), identity store | NATS is the system bus, not just a component; each box has its own JetStream domain and leaf links [C-W4-054] (N-6, AI-9) |
| *(none)* | **L2b · Devices and robots:** MCP/MHS device adapters, ROS 2 bridge, MAVLink, IIoT site bus (MQTT 5 broker bridged to NATS), TAK | Robotics, IoT and TAK are the moat (M-7, F-11, N-7); Olares has none [olares §8] |
| **Framework:** app-service, bfl, system-server, Authelia/LLDAP, backup-server, market, upgrade, headscale | **L3 · OneMind framework:** `onemindd` (update agent), Market controller (`OneMindApp` → Helm), **capability gateway** (MCP/CLI/API/SSH with gateway-enforced approvals), identity (local OIDC, works offline), backup agent, Headscale/Tailscale | Copy: owner-signed dangerous operations, per-app namespaces, linter. Do better: default-deny egress (Olares has ingress-only policies [C-TD-olares-034]), offline identity, verified restores (A-1, U-4, AI-5) |
| **Built-in apps:** Desktop, Files, Vault, Wise, Settings, Control Hub, Studio | **L4 · OneMind app shell + modules** (D-003): dashboard, files, alerts, settings, infrastructure map, family, finance, wellness, robotics, mesh/IoT, OSINT, education | Each module is also a normal Market app with its own `onemind.app.yaml` (D-003) |
| **Community apps:** OAC (Helm + `OlaresManifest.yaml`) checked by a linter [C-TD-olares-028] | **L5 · Market:** Helm + `onemind.app.yaml`, public linter, signed publishers, Compose importer | Trust by signature and provenance, never by image-name substring (the Olares linter exempts any image path containing "beclab/", which lets its own privileged agent pass [C-TD-olares-030]) (A-5, A-6) |
| AI: Router gateway, Lares agent, CLI with Agent Skills | **AI layer** on the capability gateway: local + cloud model routing, Hermes bodies on the box, versioned CLI skills | Copy the Router and CLI-skills patterns (AI-2, AI-4); never a privileged sidecar for the agent |

| *rev. 2* Flatcar Container Linux | Read-only A/B OS with signed sysext extensions bound to the OS release; per-vendor kmod sysexts with a depmod-at-merge hook [C-W19-005, C-REF-W19-cost-010]; the masking bug that hook fixed [C-REF-W19-cost-008] | funded community, x86/arm64 servers, no Pi 5, no robotics | Medium (same extension mechanism; different hardware) | DC-16's E3/E4 design and its failure modes |
| *rev. 2* Home Assistant / Umbrel / CasaOS (install paths) | All three dropped or stalled their script/BYO-distro path in favour of the image [C-REF-DC-longevity-004, C-REF-DC-longevity-005, C-REF-DC-longevity-006] | small-to-funded appliance teams | **High** | P6 sunset at image GA |
| *rev. 2* Bosch Rexroth ctrlX OS | Ubuntu Core; IEC 62443-4-2 SL2 by TÜV Rheinland [C-CIT4-012] | industrial vendor, own hardware | Medium for IIoT; low for consumer | The strongest Ubuntu-side IIoT precedent (§6.5); the industrial route stays a partner decision (Z-11) |
| *rev. 3* ZimaOS 1.8 (IceWhale) | Buildroot + signed RAUC verity bundle, GRUB A/B, 3 tries, rescue entry, offline drop folder; closed-source OS, read from the release bundle [C-TD-casaos-zimaos-008, C-TD-casaos-zimaos-012] | small company, x86 consumer NAS/home server, Docker, no k8s | **High** for the engine; low for the base (Buildroot) | A second consumer appliance independently on RAUC with 3 tries; its field failures (a slot that panicked on every try while the UI said "succeeded"; Docker knocking `rauc` off D-Bus) are why OneMind's UI reports the booted slot and the gate checks fresh signals [C-TD-casaos-zimaos-044, C-TD-casaos-zimaos-045] |
| *rev. 3* SteamOS 3 (Valve) | Arch Linux + RAUC + casync A/B [C-W18-044] | large funded vendor, one hardware family, no k8s | Low (single SKU, different base and team) | The only package-distro + RAUC product found; shows RAUC on a non-Buildroot base at scale, not on Debian |
| *rev. 3* umbrelOS (engine history) | Debian + Mender, then Debian + Rugix; still builds a Mender variant for legacy devices [C-W3-040] | small company, Pi 5 + x86 | **High** | The only constraint-matched Debian A/B precedent, and it is for Mender and Rugix, not RAUC (§9.1) |

### 9.1 N9: is there a constraint-matched production precedent for Debian + RAUC + k3s? *(rev. 3)*

**Answer: no.** We searched for a shipped product built on a Debian or Ubuntu root filesystem with RAUC as its A/B engine (three extended web searches, RAUC's site and its docs at v1.15.2, and the competitor ledger). No field-proven one was found [C-DEC-013, UNVERIFIED: negative search]. One pre-1.0 public project exists: IronFlockOS (Debian 13 + RAUC + Docker; Pi 4/5 and x86 images; Jetson on Ubuntu 24.04 in progress; no target past hardware acceptance on 2026-11-11) [C-CIT4-001, C-CIT4-002]. Watch it as the nearest precedent: if it reaches 1.0 on hardware, the S2 "Debian precedent" row should be re-scored. RAUC's own site lists build-system and distribution support but names no adopters [C-DEC-012], and its docs say the main use of RAUC packages on general-purpose distributions is to *create and inspect* bundles on a build host, not to run on devices [C-DEC-011]. The RAUC products we can name are Buildroot-based (Home Assistant OS, ZimaOS) or Arch-based (SteamOS). No product at all combines an A/B OS with k3s on a consumer appliance (Olares runs k3s with no OS A/B [C-TD-olares-007]).

| Layer | Constraint-matched precedent | Fit |
|---|---|---|
| Debian base, image as a container build, Pi 5 + x86, OTA major rebase | umbrelOS [C-W2-043, C-W3-039] | High |
| RAUC engine, verity bundles, 3 tries, appliance UX | Home Assistant OS, ZimaOS [C-LAB-rauc-010, C-TD-casaos-zimaos-012] | High for the engine; base differs |
| k3s app platform on a single appliance | Olares [C-TD-olares-021] | High for the platform; no OS A/B |
| **All three together** | **none** | — |

**What this implies, under rule 2:** no competitor pattern is the default for the *combination*; each layer has its own constraint-matched default, and assembling them is OneMind's own integration. Four consequences, all adopted:
1. **The integration is proven only by our labs.** That is now six pre-registered spikes with real k3s across two bases and three engines, plus Secure Boot in emulation; the hardware tests (F1/F2 power pulls, F-PI5, F-SB-1…3) are **release gates**, not nice-to-haves.
2. **Ship RAUC the way the precedents do:** HAOS builds and pins its own RAUC through Buildroot [C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-009]; OneMind builds its own from a tag on Debian (§4.2), rather than relying on a distribution package that RAUC's own docs say is mainly for bundle creation.
3. **In S2, the "proven competitor on a Debian base" row goes to Mender** (umbrelOS shipped Debian + Mender), which narrows RAUC's tie-break to 5–3 (§3.2). RAUC still has the stronger record *as an engine*.
4. **Keep the exit open:** the engine-neutral layout, which the Mender lab confirmed with a second engine [C-LAB-mender-008…014], means OneMind can move to the engine with the Debian precedent (Mender or Rugix) by an update, not a reflash, if the hardware gates expose a Debian-specific RAUC problem. An actual engine-migration artifact is still untested.

## 10. What the VM lab proved, and what it didn't

| Spike | Result | Claims |
|---|---|---|
| Debian 13 + Rugix (x86_64 and aarch64, QEMU) | All pre-declared tests passed in run 4 on both: good update committed after k3s passed fresh checks; bad update rolled back at the 300 s deadline; power cut mid-write survived. Found the **stale-status trap**. | C-LAB-debian-rugix-020…024 |
| Debian 13 + RAUC (x86_64, QEMU) | Passed twice: wrong-key bundle refused; good update committed; unhealthy and unbootable updates rolled back; power cut mid-write and before commit both fell back safely. Gate was a stand-in service, not k3s. | C-LAB-rauc-013…018 |
| Fedora bootc + greenboot (aarch64) | Build, upgrade, automatic rollback, rebase and data survival passed; **power cut during shutdown finalize silently downgraded a healthy box (2/2 runs)**; auto-update timer re-stages a known-bad image | C-LAB-fedora-bootc-008…015 |
| W2 Fedora IoT bootc + k3s gate | Booted green with composefs and SELinux; broken update rolled back with zero manual commands but took 32 min (time target failed) | C-W2-052, C-W2-054 |
| k3s footprint + restore (aarch64) | +936 MiB idle; +1,529 MiB under load with no OOM; SQLite and etcd restores onto fresh VMs succeeded; etcd restore needs the token passed explicitly | C-LAB-k3s-footprint-restore-007…015 |
| W4-1 k3s vs Podman | 2.50 GiB left on 4 GiB; ~2.75 GB/day writes; PASS on pre-registered thresholds | C-W4-078 |
| W8-1 NATS leaf buffering | 600/600 delivered after a hub outage, no gaps or duplicates | C-W8-050 |
| *rev. 2* RAUC + real k3s gate (x86_64, QEMU TCG) | **PASS, two runs (41 min each).** Good update committed after 3 fresh passes with a broken app ignored; k3s-won't-start and kubelet-never-runs rejected at 300 s and rolled back; kernel panic and watchdog hang tried exactly 3 times then dropped for good; power cut during write and during the first trial boot both safe (the latter retried and committed). Naive gate committed the broken kubelet in both runs. Found: k3s needs bind-mounts on a read-only root; the trial marker must be written in pre-install | C-LAB-rauc-k3s-gate-001…010 |
| *rev. 2* Secure Boot + RAUC, X-2R (x86_64, OVMF + swtpm) | **PASS, two runs.** §4.3 layout unchanged; signed per-slot UKIs via RAUC 1.13's `efi` backend with only OneMind keys; A → B, rollback, firmware refusal of tampered and unsigned UKIs with same-power-on fallback; TPM unlock on every slot and rollback; locked with Secure Boot off; signed PCR 11 bonus passed. Exploratory E1 found the **downgrade gap** (an old signed kernel booted on a newer root) | C-LAB-secureboot-rauc-011…020 |
| *rev. 2* W19 sysext/confext (Debian 13, systemd 257) | Program, udev rule, confext profile and kernel module merged and removed cleanly; level mismatch refused. Not tested: verity-signed images, Secure Boot, a RAUC slot switch, two driver packs | C-W19-004, C-W19-006…009 |
| *rev. 3* RAUC 1.15.2 + real k3s gate (x86_64; first spike with a hash-verified pre-registration) | **PASS** (run 2 all tests; run 1 all but a named driver bug). Upstream 1.15.2 built from `4fb7c798` with `bundle-formats=verity` repeated every 1.13 verdict; a signed plain bundle refused; Debian's 1.13-3+deb13u1 found CVE-patched; naive gate committed the broken kubelet again. Pi 5 tryboot kit desk-checked in an arm64 VM (one backend bug found and fixed); founder test F-PI5 written | C-LAB-rauc-1152-001…017 |
| *rev. 3* Ubuntu 26.04 leg (x86_64) | **U2 PASS** (Ubuntu's RAUC 1.15.1, two complete runs) and **U1p PASS** (1.13 + 3 upstream commits); **U1 FAIL**: RAUC 1.13 cannot install any bundle on Linux 7.0 (dm-verity status `V -`). Gate files byte-identical; same commit time as Debian in a paired run | C-LAB-ubuntu-2604-leg-001…013 |
| *rev. 3* Mender 5.2.0 standalone + same gate (x86_64) | **PASS with the predicted one-try limit** in two runs: T9 abandoned a good update after a power cut during its first boot; a gate outside the artifact was silently skipped (run 0); no trial marker needed; engine-neutral layout held | C-LAB-mender-001…014 |
| *rev. 3* DC-20 minimal image, mkosi Debian vs Buildroot (x86_64) | Measurement complete; Debian leg failed `k3s check-config` until `apparmor` was added. Buildroot 4–6× smaller, SBOM complete, incremental builds 29–102 s, but 7–18% fewer CVE IDs (threshold 50%); kernel is 73–80% of IDs in both | C-LAB-minimal-image-dc20-001…021 |

| *rev. 4* RAUC 1.15.2 + k3s gate on native arm64 (Debian 13 arm64, QEMU + HVF; fallback host pre-registered) | **PASS, two full runs** (T1–T11): good update committed in 31 s; k3s-broken and stale-Ready updates rolled back at 300 s; panic and watchdog hang tried 3 times then dropped; power cut mid-install and in the commit window both safe; naive gate committed a kubelet-less k3s again. RAUC arm64 build byte-identical to rev. 3's (reproducible). Nested KVM was unusable, and a slow host can turn a good trial into a rollback because the deadline is wall-clock. Pi 5 tryboot not covered | C-LAB-aarch64-gate-001…011 |
| *rev. 4* Secure Boot + RAUC 1.15.2 re-run (x86_64, OVMF + swtpm) | **PASS, two runs:** RAUC 1.15.2 now finds the booted slot from EFI `BootCurrent`, and its EFI variable effects matched 1.13 step for step; verity-only refused a signed plain bundle; the **downgrade gap persists** (an old signed factory UKI booted on a newer root and the gate committed it); `prevent-late-fallback` documented, untested | C-LAB-secureboot-1152-001…011 |

**Not proven (founder hardware or later):** real power pulls on the Beelink/Dell (QEMU's kill doesn't drop disk caches; RAUC + k3s gate tests F1/F2); Secure Boot on real PC firmware, Microsoft-signed option ROMs and a power cut under Secure Boot (F-SB-1…3); ~~RAUC ≥ 1.15.2 (all RAUC labs used Debian's 1.13 [C-LAB-rauc-k3s-gate-005])~~ (rev. 3–4: RAUC 1.15.2 now has labs on x86, native arm64 and under Secure Boot [C-LAB-rauc-1152-011, C-LAB-aarch64-gate-004, C-LAB-secureboot-1152-005]); a Secure Boot lab on an Ubuntu guest; the `efi` route combined with the real k3s gate and a power cut during the trial boot (§4.2); dm-verity root; Pi 5 tryboot with RAUC; Jetson NVIDIA A/B with OneMind's gate (T-W3-06); Debian 13 → 14 rebase with founder-hours (T-W3-04); a 30-day hardened-k3s soak on Pi 5 NVMe; GPU time-slicing and Isaac Sim on Debian; cross-k3s-minor rollback (T-W3-03); sysext across a RAUC slot switch and its security variant (T-W19-01), two driver packs (T-W19-02), OneMind-signed modules under lockdown (T-W19-03); meta-tegra LAB-W7-6; Mender E2/E3; two-engine backup memory test; RT under k3s on Pi 5 and two Orins. **Revision 2 removed** "RAUC with a real k3s gate" and "signed UKIs with RAUC (X-2R)" from this list: both now ran in emulation. **Revision 3 removed** "RAUC ≥ 1.15.2", "Mender E2" and "the DC-20 build-both-ways measurement", and added: the Debian 13 + backports kernel ≥ 6.19 + RAUC 1.13 failure (inferred, founder test F-U1); Mender's 3-try boot-script patch (F3, runnable in QEMU); mender-convert's own output (F4); an engine-migration artifact; Ubuntu with dracut (F-U2); a Pi 5 RAUC tryboot run on hardware (F-PI5); the PCR 15 volume check (SB-5b); an aarch64 DC-20 leg; and the corrected etcd write test T-W5-03. Every rev. 3 lab ran on a host shared with other lab VMs, so durations are indicative only.

## 12. Conditions, open items and limits of this decision

- **Protocol (rev. 4, supersedes "Interpretation stated openly"):** S1 follows the frozen rules (§3.1.6). The rev-3 pairwise rule is withdrawn and could return only through a founder decision logged in §8. **New founder decisions:** Z-38 (accept the protocol result or override it), Z-39 (does an unmet or uncosted third-party COND disqualify?), Z-40 (write the tie-break counting method, the "experimental" C2 reading and the stack scope of C4 into the rules before the next tests). Under rule 8 the run assumes the protocol result (D) until Zeus rules. `24-…` should mark the S1 item `blocked: founder-decision` (follow-up for its owner).
- **Definition of done, citations (rev. 4, per CIT4): met with listed exceptions.** Every claim ID in this file resolves to a row in `evidence/claims/`. As of 2026-11-11, the rows that are not PRIMARY, or not verified, are listed in [`evidence/critic/cit4-mapping.md`](evidence/critic/cit4-mapping.md) with their role, and this file labels them inline. They are: failure reports (forum posts, incident write-ups) that support a risk or a refuter's counter-argument, not a scorecard cell; package-index and repository metadata (PRIMARY-METADATA); two negative-search or blocked results (C-DEC-013, C-STEEL-cra-nb-017); and three DC-16 conclusions that are derived, not sourced (C-W19-047…049). No scorecard cell rests only on a SECONDARY or UNVERIFIED row. IDs that appear only inside a range (for example C-REF3-DC-technical-007, -014) carry their label in the mapping file. The master `evidence/claims.csv` is still stale (pass-1 #3).
